Localization problems of Quillen
Satya Mandal

Abstract




Abstract:
Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
Spec(A)
,
U\subseteq X
be an open subset, and
Z=X-U
.Assume that
Z
is complete intersection, with
k=\mathrm{co}\dim Z
. Consider the map

\mathfrak{q}:{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\rightarrow{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)

of the
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra. We give a description of the homotopy fiber of
\mathfrak{q}
.
Let
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)
denote the full subcategory of perfect modules
{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(X)
such that(1)
{\mathscr{F}}_{|U}=0
, (2)
grade({\mathscr{F}})=\dim_{{\mathscr{V}}(X)}{\mathscr{F}}=k
.
It turns out that the homotopy fiber of
\mathfrak{q}
is the
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra

{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)\right)
.
Likewise, we compute the homotopy fiber of the pullback map

\mathfrak{g}:{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\rightarrow{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)

of Karoubi Grothendieck-Witt bispectra. Consequently, we obtain long exact sequences of
{\mathbb{K}}
-groups and of
{\mathbb{G}}W
-groups. These results settle some of the long standing open problems.

1
Introduction

In general, refer to section
C
for notations. Throughout,
GW
abbreviates
"Grothedieck-Witt", with possible variations of fonts. For notations and background on
GW
theory, refer to section
B
. Whenever we discuss
GW
theory (also known as hermitian theory), we assume
2
is invertible in the respective category.
Section
A
establishes some background on grade and annihilators of coherent modules over quasi projective schemes. All schemes considered in this article are assumed to be noetherian.

In this article we discuss the homotopy fiber of the pullback
maps

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 22.95142pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.95142pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.56287pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.95142pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.95142pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}&in~\mathrm{Sp}\\
\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 28.4028pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.4028pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.9066pt\raise-5.18748pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{p}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.4028pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.4028pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}&in~\mathrm{BiSp}\\
\end{array}\right.

(1)

where
X
is a quasi projective scheme, over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
, and
U\subseteq X
is an open subset. Here
{\mathbb{K}}\left(-\right)\in{\mathrm{Sp}}
denotes the nonconnective
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra and
{\mathbb{G}}W(-)\in\mathrm{BiSp}
denotes the Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectra (see section
B
).
We will write
Z=X-U
. Recall the classical fundamental result that, any map
\mathfrak{f}:{\mathcal{Y}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}
of pointed spaces fits into a homotopy fibration

\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}(\mathfrak{f})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{f}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}}

leading up to a long exact sequence of homotopy groups, ending at the degree zero term (see
[
W78
]
). Therefore, the question of existence of the homotopy fibers of
\mathfrak{q}
,
\mathfrak{p}
was never in contention. Right from the inception

[
Q73
]
, it has been an open
question, how best to describe the homotopy fiber of the maps
\mathfrak{q}
(
originally, of the maps of
K
-theory spaces, then of spectra and so on
), in terms of the closed complement
Z
. The questions on homotopy fiber of
\mathfrak{p}
followed, as the
GW
theory evolved. For the
{\bf G}
-theory spaces (
meaning,
K
-theory spaces of
Coh(X)
), Quillen gave fully satisfactory description of the homotopy fibers. For regular schemes,
K
-theory coincides with
{\bf G}
-theory. Therefore, the issue of such homotopy fibers remains to be interesting only when
X
is non regular.

This project was triggered when the result (
1.1
) of Quillen came to my attention.

Theorem 1.1

(Quillen
[
G74
,
G76
]
)
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi compact scheme,
U=\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)\subseteq X
is an affine open subschemes of
X
, and
Z=X-U
. Assume that
Z
has a subscheme structure, defined by an invertible ideal sheaf
{\mathscr{I}}\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.
Let

{\mathscr{H}}=\left\{{\mathscr{F}}\in QCoh(X):{\mathscr{F}}_{|U}=0,\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}({\mathscr{F}})=1\right\}\qquad{\rm be~the~full~subcategory}.

Then there is an exact sequence

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{K}_{n}\left({\mathscr{H}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{K}_{n}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{K}_{n}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{K}_{n-1}\left({\mathscr{H}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

ending at degree zero.

It was instantly clear to me, while
\mathrm{co}\dim(X,Z)=1
, the category
{\mathscr{H}}=C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
is the category of perfect modules with support on
Z
. The
K
-theory of various categories of perfect modules were previously studied in
[
M15
,
M17
,
M20
,
M23
]
and others.

After theorem
1.1
, it was clear to the experts what to expect, in higher codimension.
Following formulation was communicated to me by Daniel Grayson, which we write as an open problem.

Open Problem 1.2
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
and
Z\subseteq X
be a closed complete intersection subset, as in
(
2.1
) and
U=X-Z
. (
One says,
Z
is cutout by a regular sequence.
) Then the sequence

\textstyle{{\bf K}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf K}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}}
\textstyle{{\bf K}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}

of
K
-theory spaces, is a homotopy fibration. By now, we think about this problem in terms of nonconnective
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectrum.

In the case,
\mathrm{co}\dim(Z)=1
the problem was stated as conjectures, in the paper of Gersten
[
G74
, pp 343]

and in the paper of Weibel
[
W89
, pp 199]
.

Using the methods developed on various categories of perfect modules (
loc. cit
), we
settle this long standing open problem (
1.2
), in complete generality.
We prove when
Z
is complete intersection, as in (
2.1
), the homotopy fiber of the map
\mathfrak{q}
is given by the
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra

{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\in{\mathrm{Sp}}
(see (
A.13
,
C.1
,) for notations).
Analogously, the homotopy fiber
of
\mathfrak{p}
is given by
{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\in{\mathrm{BiSp}}
. In other words (
4.1
,
5.6
),
there are homotopy fibrations

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.93544pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 107.44972pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}&in~\mathrm{Sp}\\
\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 42.45804pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-42.45804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[-k]}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.45804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.45804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 129.76744pt\raise-5.18748pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{p}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 147.26364pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 147.26364pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}&in~\mathrm{BiSp}\\
\end{array}\right.

(2)

where
k=\mathrm{co}\dim(Z)\geq 1
and
Z
is complete intersection. Note, we have

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[-k]}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)&if~k=0~mod~4\\
{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)^{-}&if~k=2~mod~4\\
\end{array}\right.

Further,
we prove

{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\quad{\rm is~a~homotopy~equivalence~in}~{\mathrm{Sp}}.

(3)

Note,
if
X
is noetherian and regular then
{\mathbb{M}}(X)=Coh(X)
,
{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)=Coh^{Z}(X)
.
Further note that, statements analogous to (
3
) in
GW
-theory would not even make sense, because
{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
does not have a natural duality.
However,
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
has natural dualities
M\mapsto{\mathscr{E}}xt^{k}\left(M,{\mathscr{L}}\right)
, when
k=grade(Z,X)
and
{\mathscr{L}}
is any line bundle on
X
. Because of this, our results on
GW
-theory are sharper.

As a consequence to (
2
), if
Z
is complete intersection,
\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, there are exact sequences of corresponding groups

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 33.6571pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-33.6571pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.6571pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 57.6571pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 133.00327pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 133.00327pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 207.19803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 207.19803pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n-1}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\\
\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 42.4835pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-42.4835pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{n}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)^{\pm}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.4835pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.4835pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{n}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 152.73244pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 152.73244pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{n}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 237.82997pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 237.82997pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{n-1}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)^{\pm}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\\
\end{array}\right.

(4)

while we assume
\mathrm{co}\dim Z
is even for the latter exact sequence to make sense.

Thomason developed a complete throry
[
TT90
]
, in terms of
K
-theory of perfect complexes.
After the
work of Waldhausen
[
W83
]
, followed by
[
TT90
]
,
K
-theory spaces and spectra became invariants of the corresponding chain complex categories. Eventually, it percolated to the
K
-theory of perfect complexes
[
TT90
]
. The homotopy fiber of
\mathfrak{q}
is described as
K
-theory of

{\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)
in
[
TT90
]
(
see §
C
for notation
).
This description of the homotopy fiber of
\mathfrak{q}
in
[
TT90
]
was label as a "revolutionary advance"
[
TT90
, pp 337]
compared to Theorem
1.1
.
However,
I believe that
K
-theory should be a story of exact categories, which is what we do in this article.

We insert the following example, for perspective.

Example 1.3

(Deligne
[
G74
]
)
.

Let
(A,\mathfrak{m})
be a local noetherian commutative ring, which is not Cohen Macaulay.
Let
X=\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
,
Z=V(\mathfrak{m})
and
U=X-Z
. In this case,
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)=\{0\}
.
There is an example of a non Cohen Macaualay ring
A
, suggested by Deligne
[
G74
]
, such that
{\bf K}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\neq{\bf K}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)
. Therefore,
the sequence

\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}

(5)

is not a homotopy fibration. However, Thomason computes
[
TT90
]
the homotopy fiber of
\mathfrak{q}
, as
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)
, which is non trivial because
\mathfrak{q}
is not an equivalence. It would be hard to imagine a subcategory
{\mathscr{H}}\subseteq Coh(X)
, such that
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\cong{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{H}}\right)
. Note, while
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)=0
, there is no natural functor

Coh^{Z}(X)\longrightarrow{\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)
.

Differences between our results and that in
[
TT90
]
is clarified in (
1.3
). While the spectrum
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)
is a description of homotopy fiber of
\mathfrak{q}
, it does not answer the question
1.2
. To respond to Problem
1.2
, we need a subcategory
{\mathscr{H}}\subseteq Coh(X)
and an equivalence
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\cong{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{H}}\right)
, which is essentially impossible in the case of
(
1.3
). In
[
TT90
,
S17
]
there is no natural candidate to take the place of
{\mathscr{H}}
.
When
Z
is complete intersection, we establish that
{\mathscr{H}}=C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
will do the job, and we establish in the Agreement theorems (
6.10
,
6.11
) that

{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\cong{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)
and
{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\cong{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)
, as two homotopy fibers must agree.
In particular, in this article, we settle the conjecture of
Gersten
[
G74
, pp 343]

and of Weibel
[
W89
, pp 199]
, which is the case
\mathrm{co}\dim(Z)=1
of (
1.2
); along with the problem
1.2
in all codimensions
\mathrm{co}\dim(Z)\geq 1
.

As usual, work on
GW
-theory follows the advancements in
K
-theory
[
W83
,
TT90
]
. We prove the
GW
analogues of the
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory conjecture. In
[
S17
]
,

{\mathbb{G}}W
-theory was developed in the framework is dg categories. As far as this article is concerned, all we need to know is, for an exact category
{\mathscr{E}}
, the category
Ch^{b}\left(\mathscr{E}\right)
of bounded complexes in
{\mathscr{E}}
has a dg category structure, which we denote by
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}
. If
{\mathscr{E}}:=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varphi\right)
has a duality, then
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}
is a dg category with duality and weak equivalences (the quasi isomorphisms). Due to the shift funtor
T
on
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}
, we obtain one shifted
{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\in\mathrm{BiSp}
Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum, for each
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. However,
{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)
has four periodicity. Further,
{\mathscr{E}}
itself can be considered as a
dg category, concentrated at degree zero, which has its Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum.
In summary, we have

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right):={\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)={\mathbb{G}}W^{[0]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\\
{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)^{-}:={\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},-\varpi\right)={\mathbb{G}}W^{[2]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\\
\end{array}\right.

In section
B
we give an overview of all these on Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum. In this introduction, we only stated our results on
{\mathbb{G}}W\left(-\right)^{\pm}
above (
2
). We refer to the main body of this article for results on

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}-\right)
.

In section
6
we give a number of applications of our main
results (
2
), both on
{\mathbb{K}}
theory and
{\mathbb{G}}W
theory. These include (1) Cohen Macaulay local rings
(A,\mathfrak{m})
, (2) The case, when
Z=\left\{\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2},\ldots,m_{\ell}\right\}
is a set closed points, which is complete intersection. (3) The case when
U
is regular, (4) Agreement with Thomason’s description on
{\mathbb{K}}
theory fiber of
\mathfrak{q}
, and Agreement with
Schlichting’s description on Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
theory fiber of
\mathfrak{p}
, (5) Excision, (6) Mayer-Vietories.

For completeness of the story we pose the following conjecture.

Conjecture 1.4
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
, and
d=\dim X
. Let
U
be an open subset and
Z=X-U
. Assume that
Z
has a subscheme structure, such that
Z\hookrightarrow X
is a regular embedding, with
k=\mathrm{co}\dim Z=grade(Z,X)
. Then the sequences

(6)

are homotopy fibrations, in the respective categories.

Question of localization has always been an open problem among the expert, and its absence was always missed. The description in
[
TT90
]
did not level it up with the formulation in (
1.1
).
It would be hard to imagine that Quillen himself only had bank sheet,
beyond the case of codimension one, in spite of Theorem

1.1
. Again, in the light of (
1.1
), it is reasonable to speculate the Quillen’s formulation would been the same as (
6
).
For this reason I would refer to this conjecture as the
localization conjecture of Quillen
.
To the best of our search there is no documented statement on
the localization problem. The results in this article is an evidence in support of this conjecture. Secondly, regular embedding has always been a gold standard in many facets of mathematics. For example, intersection products behave fairly well for regular embeddings
Z\hookrightarrow X

[
F84
, Chap 6]
.

Regarding the organization of this article, most notations are summarized in section
C
. Notations and overview on hermitian theory are in section
B
. Some background on scheme theory (grades, annihilators, perfect modules) is in section

A
. In section
2
, we construct certain maps from some Koszul complexes, to certain
complexes of modules, in order to approximate homologies, by perfect modules.
This section
2
is the heart of the technical machineries, that are used in this article.
In section
3
we establish equivalences of certain derived categories, which are
used to prove our main results. Our main results on
{\mathbb{K}}
theory fiber are in section

4
, and the main results on Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
theory fiber are in section

5
. Application are dealt with in section
6
.

2
Koszul construction of Foxby

In this section we prove out main technical machinery.
Throughout this article we will be working with the following setup.

Setup 2.1
.

As in section
C
, let

X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
. In particular,
S=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}S_{n}
is a graded noetherian ring and
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
,
and
X\subseteq Y
is open (and dense). In general
Z\subseteq X
will denote a closed subset,
and
U=X-Z
.

Our focus will be when
Z=V(\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k})\cap X
, where
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}\in S
are homogenous.
Further,
\mathfrak{f}_{i_{1}},\mathfrak{f}_{i_{2}},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{i_{j}}
induce regular sequences on
X
,

\forall~1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{j}\leq k
. (
This means that the corresponding Koszul complexes on
X
is exact at all degrees higher than zero.
) We will say that
Z
is
complete intersection
(with
grade(Z,X)=k
or
\mathrm{co}\dim(Z)=k
).

We recall the following Koszul complex construction.

Construction 2.2
.

Let
S=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}S_{n}
be a graded noetherian ring and
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
.
Let
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{k}\in S
be homogeneous elements, with
\deg(f_{i})=r_{i}
.
The Koszul complex
K_{\bullet}(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{k})
is constructed, as usual, while we view at
it as a complex of graded free (shifted) modules, and of graded
S
-module maps of degree zero. More explicitly, consider the graded free
S
-module

P=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}S(-r_{i})e_{i}\quad{\rm and~the~map}\quad\varphi:P\longrightarrow S,\quad{\rm defined~by}\quad\varphi(e_{i})=f_{i}\in S.

So,
\varphi\in Hom(P,S)
is a map of degree zero. Therefore,

K_{\bullet}\left(\varphi\right)=K_{\bullet}\left(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{k}\right)

is the Koszul complex of
\varphi
. In particular, the degree
p
-term of
K_{\bullet}(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{k})
is given by

\Lambda^{p}P=\bigoplus_{1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{p}\leq k}S\left(-(r_{i_{1}}+r_{i_{2}}+\cdots+r_{i_{p}})\right)e_{i_{1}}\wedge e_{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{i_{p}}

Sheafifying, we obtain the Koszul complex

{\mathscr{K}}_{\bullet}\left(\widetilde{\varphi}\right)={\mathscr{K}}_{\bullet}\left(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{k}\right)
of locally free sheaves, on
Y
. Again, the degree
p
-term is given by

\Lambda^{p}\widetilde{P}=\bigoplus_{1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{p}\leq k}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\left(-(r_{i_{1}}+r_{i_{2}}+\cdot+r_{i_{p}})\right)e_{i_{1}}\wedge e_{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{i_{p}}

The complex is exact, only at the points
\wp\in Y
, such that
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{k}
induce
S_{(\wp)}
-regular sequences.

The following is an improvisation of a construction of Foxby
[
F82
]
.

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{B_{r-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{r-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Write
e=e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{r}
. We have

\nu_{r-1}^{r-1}\kappa_{r}^{r-1}(e)=\sum\pm f_{i}^{r-1}\nu_{r-1}^{r-1}(\hat{e_{i}})=0\quad{\rm So,}\quad\nu_{r-1}^{r-1}\kappa_{r}^{r-1}(e)\in Z_{r-1}\left(M_{\bullet}\right)\quad{\rm is~a~cycle.}

Therefore

\nu_{r}^{r}\kappa_{r}^{r}(e)=\det(\Delta)\left(\nu_{r-1}^{r-1}\kappa(e)\right)\in B_{r-1}\left(M_{\bullet}\right)\quad{\rm is~a~boundary.}

So, we can pick

m_{12\cdots r}\in M_{r}\qquad\ni\qquad d_{r}\left(m_{12\cdots r}\right)=\nu_{r}^{r}\kappa_{r}^{r}(e)

Define

\nu_{r}^{r}(e)=m_{12\cdots r}

This settles the inductive step, for
m=r
. For
m\geq r+1
,
\nu_{\bullet}^{m}
is defined
by the consistency diagram
(
7
).

This completes the inductive case.
The proof is complete.

Now we do the same (
2.3
) for projective schemes
Y
.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
be projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Let

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{k-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{0}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{-1}}

(8)

be a bounded complex of coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
-module.
Write
M=\bigoplus{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)
and
I\subseteq S
be homogeneous ideal such that
\tilde{I}=ann(M)
. Let
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}\in I
be homogenous elements. Then there is a map of complexes

\nu_{\bullet}:{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}
such that

1.

First,
{\mathscr{E}}_{i}\in{\mathscr{V}}(Y)~\forall~i
, and
{\mathscr{E}}_{i}=0
unless
0\leq i\leq k
.

2.

The map
\nu_{0}:{\mathscr{E}}_{0}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)
is surjective, and hence

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}(\nu_{\bullet}):{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)
is surjective.

3.

In fact,
{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}
will be the direct sum of certain Koszul complexes, similar to the map
(
7
). And

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\otimes{\mathcal{L}}=\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}{\widetilde{\left(\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{m},\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}^{m}\right)}}\right)^{t}\quad m\gg 0,~for~some~t

(9)

and for some line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}
(i.e.
up to a twist
).

4.

Assume
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}
induce an regular sequence on an open subset
X\subseteq Y
.
Then

\left({\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)_{|X}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\qquad with\quad Z=V\left(\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}\right)\cap X.

Proof.
As the diagram (
17
) suggets, for fixed
k
and
m\geq k
we will construct one map
\nu_{\bullet}^{m}
and
\nu_{\bullet}:=\nu_{\bullet}^{m}
will be a map, as requited.
By twisting (
8
) high enough, we assume
{\mathscr{F}}_{i}
, the boundaries

{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)
, the cycles
{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)
, and the homologies
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)
are globally generated and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{H}^{i}\left(Y,{\mathscr{F}}_{i}\right)=0&\forall i\geq 1\\
{H}^{i}\left(Y,{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)=0&\forall i\geq 1,\forall j\\
{H}^{i}\left(Y,{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)=0&\forall i\geq 1,\forall j\\
{H}^{i}\left(Y,{\mathcal{H}}_{j}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)=0&\forall i\geq 1,\forall j\\
\end{array}\right.\qquad\qquad{\rm\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{H}{}{}, III.5.2, III.5.3]}}

Let
z\in H^{0}\left(Y,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)
and let

\mu_{z}:{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\subseteq{\mathscr{F}}_{0}
be the map induced by
1\mapsto z
.
By induction on
k
, we prove that for any selection of
1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{r}\leq k
, there is a
map of complexes

\nu_{\bullet}^{r}:{\mathcal{K}}_{\bullet}\left(\mathfrak{f}_{i_{1}}^{m},\mathfrak{f}_{i_{2}}^{m},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{i_{r}}^{m}\right)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\qquad\forall m\geq r

(10)

1.

This Koszul complex

{\mathcal{K}}_{\bullet}\left(\mathfrak{f}_{i_{1}}^{m},\mathfrak{f}_{i_{2}}^{m},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{i_{r}}^{m}\right)={\mathcal{K}}_{\bullet}\left(\widetilde{\varphi^{r}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{r}}}\right)
is built
from the map of graded modules
\varphi^{r}_{i_{1}\ldots i_{r}}:F_{i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{r}}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}Se_{i_{j}}\longrightarrow S
sending

e_{i}\mapsto\mathfrak{f}_{i}^{m}
.

2.

\nu_{0}^{r}=\mu_{z}

3.

With the map

\nu_{k-1}^{k-1}\kappa^{k-1}
factors through

. The exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{0}

induces the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\Gamma_{*}\left(X,{\mathcal{H}}_{r-1}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Note that the map
\det(\Delta)
is induced by the product
\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{f}_{2}\cdots\mathfrak{f}_{k}
.
Since
\mathfrak{f}_{i}\in I

it follows that the
\det(\Delta)\eta
factors thorough a map
\zeta:{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(-kd)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}_{k-1}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)
, as in diagram (
14
).
We further have the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{r-1}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

This induces the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{*}\left(Y,{\mathcal{Z}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{*}\left(Y,{\mathscr{F}}_{r}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{*}\left(Y,{\mathcal{B}}_{r-1}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

The map
\zeta
is induced by a map
\omega:S{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow H^{0}\left(Y,{\mathscr{B}}_{k-1}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)(kd)\right)
. Write

\omega(\mathfrak{e})=\varepsilon\in H^{0}\left(Y,{\mathscr{B}}_{k-1}(kd)\right)
. Now we make a choice

m_{123\ldots k}\in H^{0}\left(Y,{\mathscr{F}}_{k}(kd)\right)\qquad\ni\qquad\mathfrak{q}(m_{123\ldots k})=\varepsilon

Define

\nu^{k}_{k}:{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}(-kd)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{k}\qquad{\rm by}\qquad\nu_{k}^{k}(\mathfrak{e})=m_{123\ldots k}

This completes the construction of (
10
), as hypothesized.

Note, we assumed
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)
is generated by finitely many global sections

z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{t}\in H^{0}\left(Y,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)
.
We construct and define
t
maps :

\nu_{\bullet}^{k,j}:{\mathcal{K}}\left(\mathfrak{f}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}\right)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\qquad\qquad\nu_{0}^{k,j}(1)=z_{j}

Taking direct sum

\nu_{\bullet}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{t}\nu_{k}^{k,j}:{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}:=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{t}{\mathcal{K}}\left(\mathfrak{f}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}\right)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}

With
\nu_{\bullet}=\nu_{\bullet}^{n}
with
n\geq k
the proposition is established. To see this, first we have

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)=\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{{\widetilde{(\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{n},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}^{n})}}}\right)^{t}\quad{\rm in~this~case,~after~some~twist.}

(15)

So, (
3
) holds up to some twist. Likewise, (
1
,
2
) in this twisted case, and hence they hold after twisting back, as well.

Finally, since
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}
induce a regular sequence on
X
, it follows from
(
15
) that
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)_{|X}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. So, (
3
) is also established.
The proof is complete.

Before we do the quasi projective version of (
2.4
), we insert the following extension of
[
H77
, Ex. II.5.15]
.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
Y
be a noetherian scheme and
\iota:X\hookrightarrow Y
be an open subset. Let
{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
be a complex coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules, as follows:

\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{k+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{k}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{r}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{r-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{-1}}

Consider the direct image complex
\iota_{*}{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
, which is a complex of quasi coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
-modules. Then there is a sub complex
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\subseteq\iota_{*}{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
of coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
-modules, which extends
{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
.

Proof.
By
[
H77
, Ex II.1.15]
, for
r=-1,0,\ldots,k,k+1
, there are coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
-modules
{F}_{r}\subseteq\iota_{*}{\mathscr{G}}_{r}
which extends
{\mathscr{G}}_{r}
. However the direct image
\iota_{*}\partial_{r}
may not restrict to a map from
{F}_{r}
to
F_{r-1}
. To correct this, inductively we define

{\mathscr{F}}_{k+1}=F_{k+1},{\mathscr{F}}_{k}=F_{k}+\iota_{*}\partial_{k+1}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{k+1}\right),\ldots,{\mathscr{F}}_{r}=F_{k}+\iota_{*}\partial_{r+1}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{r+1}\right),\cdots

For open subsets
U\subseteq X
, inductively, we have

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathscr{F}}_{k+1}(U)=F_{k+1}(U)={\mathscr{G}}_{k+1}(U)\\
{\mathscr{F}}_{k}(U)=F_{k}(U)+\partial_{k+1}{\mathscr{G}}_{k+1}(U)={\mathscr{G}}_{k}(U)\\
\cdots\\
{\mathscr{F}}_{r}(U)=F_{r}(U)+\partial_{r+1}{\mathscr{G}}_{k+1}(U)={\mathscr{G}}_{r}(U)\\
\cdots\\
\end{array}\right.

The proof is complete.

The following is the main result in this section, which is a quasi projective version of (
2.4
).

Theorem 2.6
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
and let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset, with
grade(Z,X)=k
.
Let

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{k-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{0}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{-1}}

(16)

be a complex in
Coh(X)
, such that the homologies
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\in Coh^{Z}(X)~\forall i=0,1,\ldots,k
. Then there is a sequence
\mathfrak{f}_{1},f_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}
on
X
such that
Z\subseteq V(\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k})
and a map of complexes

\nu_{\bullet}:{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
such that

1.

First,
{\mathscr{E}}_{i}\in{\mathscr{V}}(X)~\forall i
, and
{\mathscr{E}}_{i}=0
unless
0\leq i\leq k
.

2.

The map
\nu_{0}:{\mathscr{E}}_{0}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)
is surjective, and hence

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}(\nu_{\bullet}):{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)
is also surjective.

3.

In fact,
{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}
will be the direct sum of certain Koszul complexes, similar to the map
(
7
). And

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\otimes{\mathcal{L}}=\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\widetilde{\left(\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{m},\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}^{m}\right)_{|X}}}\right)^{t}\quad m\gg 0,~for~some~t

(17)

and for some line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}
(i.e.
up to a twist
).

4.

Finally, if
Z=V\left(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{k}\right)\cap X
complete intersection as in Setup
2.1
, then further

\left({\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)_{|X}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
.

Proof.
We assume
{\mathscr{G}}_{n}=0,~n\geq k+2
, because there is no loss of generality.
We will complete the prrof, by an application of (
2.4
).
We will deal with (
4
) at the end and prove the rest first. Assume
X\subseteq Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open and dense,
where
S=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}S_{n}=A[x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{N}]
, with
\deg{x_{i}}=1
.
Let
Z=\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell}V(\wp_{j})\cap X
be an irreducible decomposition,
with
\wp_{j}\in X
and
J=\bigcap_{j=1}^{\ell}{\wp}_{j}
.

By (
2.5
) there is a subcomplex

{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\subseteq\iota_{*}{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
of coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
-modules such that the restriction

{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}}_{|X}={\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
. Since
{\iota_{*}{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}}_{|Y-V(J)}=0
, we have
{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}}_{|Y-V(J)}=0
. It follows that
\mathrm{Supp}\left(\bigoplus{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)\subseteq V(J)
. Let
I\subseteq S
be a homogeneous ideal such that
\tilde{I}=ann\left(\bigoplus{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)
.
So,
V(I)\subseteq V(J)
and hence
J^{p}\subseteq I
, for
p\gg 0
. Further

{\mathscr{I}}:=\left(\bigoplus{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)=\tilde{I}_{|X}
.

Inductively, we pick
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}\in J^{p}
that induce an
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-regular sequences on
X
.

1.

Let
\mathfrak{p}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{n}
be the associated primes of
S
, such that
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\in X
.
So,
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
corresponds to associated primes of
S_{(f_{j})}
. Suppose
J\subseteq\mathfrak{p}_{i}
. This is impossible because
grade(\left(J_{(\mathfrak{p}_{i})},{\mathcal{O}}_{X,\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right)\geq k
. So, we can pick a homogeneous element
\mathfrak{f}_{1}\in J^{p}-\bigcup\mathfrak{p}_{i}
.

2.

Now assume
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{u-1}\in J^{p}
, with
u\leq r
, has been selected so that, any of its subsequence, will induce a
regular
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-sequence. Let

\mathcal{P}=\left\{{\wp}\in X:\wp\in Ass\left(\frac{S}{(\mathfrak{f}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{i_{v}})}\right):v\leq u-1,1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{v}\leq u-1\right\}

Suppose
J\subseteq{\wp}\in{\mathcal{P}}
. This is impossible because

grade\left(J_{(\wp)},S_{(\wp)}\right)\geq k
. So, we pick

\mathfrak{f}_{u}\in J^{p}-\bigcup\left\{\wp:\wp\in{\mathcal{P}}\right\}\qquad{\rm where~}\mathfrak{f}_{u}~{\rm is~homogeneous.}

So, the existence of
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}\in J^{p}
is established.
In the case of point (
4
), we do not go through this process, and instead use

\mathfrak{f}_{1}:=f_{1}^{p},~\mathfrak{f}_{2}:=f_{2}^{p},~\ldots,~\mathfrak{f}_{k}=f_{k}^{p}\in J^{p}\subseteq I

which is also a regular sequence on
X
, by hypothesis.
Apply (
2.4
) to the extended complex
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}
. This way we obtain a map
\nu_{\bullet}:{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}
, satisfying all the properties in (
2.4
).
Now the theorem follows by restricting
\nu_{\bullet}
to
X
.
The proof is complete.

The following small improvement of (
2.6
) will be some use in future.

Corollary 2.7
.

Consider the hypotheses of (
2.6
). Further assume that

{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\in Coh_{Z}(X)
. Then the map
\nu_{\bullet}:{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
in (
2.6
), can be constructed with the additional property that that
\nu_{0}
factors as follows:

\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proof.
Use the notations as in the proof of (
2.6
,
2.4
). We outline the adjustments needed to proof of (
2.6
). There is no loss of generality, if we assume

{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)={\mathscr{G}}_{0}
. Let
K\subseteq S
be a homogeneous ideal, such that
\tilde{K}=ann\left({\mathscr{F}}_{0}\right)
. It follows

\mathrm{Supp}\left({\mathscr{F}}\oplus\left(\bigoplus{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)\right)\subseteq V(J)
. So,

V(K\cap I)\subseteq V(J)
and hence

for some

. So, the choices of
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{f}_{k}\in J^{p}\subseteq K\cap I
.

Now we do the adjustment needed in the proof of (
2.4
). In the diagram
(
11
), we have
\mathfrak{f}_{1}z=0
. It follows from this that the diagram (
12
) can be extended to the following

1

(18)

Therefore, the diagram

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 54.20033pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.20033pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad{\rm commutes}\quad\forall m\geq k

The map
\nu_{0}^{m}
is obtained by taking direct sum of such diagrams. This establishes the corollary. The proof is complete.

The following basic result is a consequence of (
2.6
), which is a refinement of
[
M15
, Lem 22]
.

Corollary 2.8
.

As in (
2.6
), let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
,
and
Z\subseteq X
be as in the setup (
2.1
). Let
{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh^{Z}(X)
. Then there is a surjective map

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 6.40279pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.09375pt\raise 5.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{f}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad{\rm with}\qquad{\mathscr{E}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X).

Proof.
It is immediate application of (
2.6
) to the complex

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

. The proof is complete.

3
Derived Equivalences

In this section, we prove two equivalence theorems (
3.2
,
3.11
) and summarize them in Theorem
3.12
.
Before we proceed we recall the following from
[
M15
, Lem 3.2]
that is needed to prove the first equivalence Theorem
3.2
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
{\mathscr{A}}
be an abelian category and
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
be two objects in the bounded derived category
{\bf D}^{b}({\mathscr{A}})
. Assume, for some
n_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{H}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)=0&\forall r\leq n_{0}-1\\
{\mathcal{H}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)=0&\forall r\geq n_{0}\\
\end{array}\right.

Then
Mor_{{\bf D}^{b}({\mathscr{A}})}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)=0
. Further, if
{\mathscr{V}}\subseteq{\mathscr{A}}
is a
resolving subcategory, then the same holds for
{\bf D}^{b}({\mathscr{V}})
.

The following is the statement of our first equivalence theorem.

Theorem 3.2
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in section
C
. So,
X\subseteq Y:=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open, as in (
C.2
).
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset.
Consider the commutative diagram of natural functors

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{Z}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({{\mathbb{M}}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({{\mathscr{V}}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{Z}}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta}

Then
\zeta
and
\zeta_{Z}
are are natural equivalences.
Further, if
Z
is complete intersection with
grade(Z,X)=k
, as in the setup (
2.1
) then
\iota_{Z}
is also a natural equivalence.

Proof.
It is well known that the functor
\zeta:{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
is an
equivalence (e.g. one can use
[
K99
, Sec. 1.5]
). Restricting this equivalence
to
{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)
, it is clear that

\zeta_{Z}
is an equivalence. For future reference we comment that inverse of
\zeta_{Z}
is given by the double complex trick.

So, now we prove that
\iota_{Z}
is an equivalence, when
Z
is a complete intersection as in (
2.1
).
We write the proof in the following propositions. See remark
3.5
.

The following is an analogue of
[
M15
, Prop 3.4]
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Use the notations as in Theorem
3.2
. Assume
Z
is complete intersection with
grade(Z,X)=k
, as in (
2.1
). Then,

\iota_{Z}
is essentially surjective and full.

Proof.
We will write
\iota:=\iota_{Z}
. Note that
\iota
is an inclusion, at object level.
For
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in Ch^{b}(Coh(X))
, we say

width\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\leq r,\quad if\quad{\mathcal{H}}_{p}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)=0,~~\forall~p\notin[n,n+r],\quad{\rm for~some}\quad n\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

We use induction on the width
r
, and prove the following:

1.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
and

width\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\leq r
. Then
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\cong\iota_{Z}\left(\tilde{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\bullet}\right)
, for some
\tilde{{\mathscr{F}}}_{\bullet}\in Obj\left({\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)
.

2.

Given
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\in Obj\left({\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)
, with
width\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\bigoplus{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\leq r
, the map

Mor_{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\twoheadrightarrow Mor_{{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)

is surjective.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
and
width\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\leq r=0
. We can assume
n=0
and hence

{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)=0,~\forall~i\neq 0
. By replacing
{\mathscr{F}}_{0}
by

Z_{0}=\ker\left({\mathscr{F}}_{0}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{-1}\right)\in{\mathbb{M}}(X)
, we can assume that
{\mathscr{F}}_{i}=0,~\forall i\leq-1
. Note,
B_{0}=Image\left({\mathscr{F}}_{1}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{0}\right)\in{\mathbb{M}}(X)
. So,

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)=\frac{Z_{0}}{B_{0}}\in{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
.
As usual, we treat
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)
as a complex in
{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
,
concentrated at degree zero.
It follows that
the map
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\iota\left({\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)
is a quasi isomorphism, in
Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
. So, (
1
) is established, when
r=0
.

Now suppose
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\in Obj\left({\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)

and

width\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\bigoplus{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\leq r=0
. We can assume

{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)={\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)=0\quad\forall~i\neq 0

Let
f_{\bullet}\in Mor_{{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)
. We can write

f_{\bullet}=g_{\bullet}t_{\bullet}^{-1}:\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.56252pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 32.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{W}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.8993pt\raise 5.65279pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.65279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t_{\bullet}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.66338pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g_{\bullet}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.43755pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.43755pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad{\rm where}\quad t_{\bullet}~~{\rm is~a~quasi~isomorphism~in}~Ch^{b}\left(({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)

As before, we can assume
{\mathscr{F}}_{i}={\mathscr{W}}_{i}={\mathscr{G}}_{i}=0~\forall i\leq-1
.
Now, the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{W}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{g_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{W}}_{\bullet}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left(t_{\bullet}\right)}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left(g_{\bullet}\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)}

gives a lift of
f_{\bullet}
.
In other words,

f_{\bullet}=\iota\left(\beta^{-1}{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left(g_{\bullet}\right){\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left(t_{\bullet}\right)^{-1}\alpha\right)
.
So, (
2
) is established, when
r=0
.

For the inductive step, assume
r\geq 1
. Let

{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in Obj({\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
, with
width\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\leq r
.
We can assume

{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)=0\quad unless~~r\geq i\geq 0\quad{\rm and}\quad{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\neq 0,\quad\mathrm{Supp}\left({\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)\subseteq Z\quad\forall i.

As before, we can assume that
{\mathscr{F}}_{i}=0,~\forall~i\leq-1
.
Since
Z
is complete intersection, with
grade(Z,X)=k
, by Theorem
2.6
(
4
),
there is a morphism
\nu_{\bullet}:{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}
such that

1.

the complex
{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\in{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)
,

{\mathscr{E}}_{i}=0
unless
0\leq i\leq k:=grade(Z,X)
.

2.

{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)=0,~\forall i\neq 0
.

3.

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
.

4.

Finally, the map
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left(\nu_{\bullet}\right):{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)
is surjective.

As before, we consider

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\in{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
,
concentrated at degree zero. Then
\iota\left({\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
.
Embed
\nu_{\bullet}
in an exact
triangle

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 16.6719pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.6719pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T^{-1}\Delta_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.06796pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\mu_{\bullet}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.6719pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.6719pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.91034pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\nu_{\bullet}}$}}}\kern (21)

Here
\eta_{\bullet}
in
{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
is obtained, by properties of triangulated categories. The induction hypothesis applies to
\eta_{\bullet}
. So, there are morphisms

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\tilde{\varphi}_{\bullet}:=\varphi_{\bullet}:{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{L}}_{\bullet}\right)\\
\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}:\Delta_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\bullet}\\
\end{array}\right.\quad{\rm in}\quad{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)~~\ni~~\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\iota\left(\tilde{\varphi}_{\bullet}\right)=\varphi_{\bullet}\\
\iota\left(\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}\right)=\eta_{\bullet}\\
\end{array}\right.

Now consider the diagram

\textstyle{T^{-1}\Delta_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(22)

It is clear that the left hand rectangle commutes in
{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(Z)\right)
. By Lemma
3.1
, it follows that the left hand square commutes in
{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
. By properties of
the triangulated category
{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
, it follows that there is a map

g_{\bullet}:{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
in
{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
, so that the above
diagram commutes.

Now we claim that
f_{\bullet}
is in the image of
\iota
. Write
h=f_{\bullet}-\iota\left(g_{\bullet}\right)
. Apply
\iota
to the diagram (
22
) and take the difference with diagram (
21
). This way we obtain the following diagram:

\textstyle{T^{-1}\Delta_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\nu}_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\omega}
\textstyle{\Delta_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon}
\textstyle{T^{-1}\Gamma_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{L}}_{\bullet}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\mu}_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{\bullet}}

The maps
\omega
and
\varepsilon
are obtained by weak kernel and weak cokernel properties.

1.

Consider the case,
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)=0
. By Lemma

3.1

Mor_{{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\bullet}\right)\right)=0
. So,
\omega=0
and hence
h=0
. Therefore,
\iota\left(g_{\bullet}\right)=f_{\bullet}
.
So, the proposition is established whenever
width\left({\mathscr{F}}\bigoplus{\mathscr{G}}\right)\leq r
and

{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)=0
.

2.

Consider the case,
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)\neq 0
.
In this case,
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\left(\Delta_{\bullet}\right)=0
,
and
width\left(\Delta_{\bullet}\bigoplus{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\leq r
. So, by the above case,

Mor_{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)}\left(\Delta_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\twoheadrightarrow Mor_{{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)}\left(\Delta_{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\right)\qquad{\rm is~surjective.}

Therefore
\iota(\tilde{\varepsilon})=\varepsilon
,
for some
\tilde{\varepsilon}
.
Hence
h=\iota(\tilde{\varepsilon}\gamma)
is in the image of
\iota
. Hence
f_{\bullet}=\iota(g_{\bullet})+h=\iota\left((g_{\bullet}+\tilde{\varepsilon}\gamma\right)
is in the image of
\iota
.

The proof is complete.

Now we prove the faithfulness of
\iota_{z}
.

Proposition 3.4
.

Use the notations, as in (
3.2
), and let
Z
is complete intersection as in (
2.1
). Then the functor

\iota:=\iota_{Z}
is faithful.

Proof.
Let
f_{\bullet}:{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}
is a map in
{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
,
and
\iota_{Z}\left(f_{\bullet}\right)=0
. We prove
f_{\bullet}=0
. We can assume that

f_{\bullet}
is denominator free. So,
f_{\bullet}
is a morphism in
Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
. Embed
f_{\bullet}
in
an exact triangle

\textstyle{T^{-1}\Delta_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{\Delta_{\bullet}}

Applying
\iota_{Z}
, we obtain the exact triangle

124.2414pt\raise-31.98666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Delta_{\bullet}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad{\rm in}\quad{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right).

By fullness (
3.3
), there is

\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}\in Mor_{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},T^{-1}\Delta_{\bullet}\right)\qquad\ni\qquad\iota_{Z}\left(\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}\right)=\eta_{\bullet}.

Now we embed
g_{\bullet}\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}
in an exact triangle

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.56252pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.52792pt\raise 7.36111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g_{\bullet}\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.68755pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.68755pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gamma_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 114.53477pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 114.53477pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad{\rm in}\quad{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right).

Under
\iota_{Z}
, this exact triangle maps to

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.56252pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.56946pt\raise 5.25555pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25555pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{1}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.56252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.68755pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.68755pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gamma_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 114.53477pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 114.53477pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad{\rm in}\quad{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right).

This means that
\Gamma_{\bullet}\cong 0
is acyclic in
{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
. In other words,
it is exact, and hence is acyclic (exact) in
{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
, as well. It follows now that
g_{\bullet}\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}
is an isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
.
Therefore
g_{\bullet}\left(\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}\left(g_{\bullet}\tilde{\eta}_{\bullet}\right)^{-1}\right)=1_{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}}
. So,
g_{\bullet}
splits in
{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
. Since

f_{\bullet}g_{\bullet}=0
, it follows

f_{\bullet}=0
in
{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)
.
The proof is complete.

Remark 3.5
.

This completes the proof of Theorem
3.2
.

3.1
The category
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
of perfect modules

The importance of various categories of perfect modules in
K
-theory was already evident in
[
M15
,
M17
,
M23
]
and others. In this article, we further work with that category of perfect modules
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
, with support in
Z
. Its importance in hermitian theory is particularly crucial, because it
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
has a natural duality.
In this subsection, we prove the equivalence

{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\cong{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)

of the derived categories, under the setup (
2.1
).
We augment the equivalence in Theorem
3.2
, with this further equivalence.
First, we establish some preparatory work.

Lemma 3.6
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
So,
X\subseteq Y:=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open.
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset and
grade(Z,X)=k
. Consider the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Suppose
{\mathscr{E}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)
and
{\mathscr{F}}\in{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)
.
Then
\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{K}}}\leq\max\left\{k,\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}-1\right\}
. In case
\max\left\{k,\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}-1\right\}=\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}-1
then
\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{K}}}=\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}-1
.

Proof.
Use Tor argument, locally.

We record some basic properties of the category
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
, as follows.

Lemma 3.7
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
So,
X\subseteq Y:=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open.
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset and
grade(Z,X)=k
.

1.

Suppose
{\mathscr{E}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
and
{\mathscr{L}}\in{\mathscr{V}}(X)
.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}\otimes{\mathscr{L}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
.

2.

Suppose
{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh^{Z}(X)
and
Z
is complete intersection as in (
2.1
). Then there is a surjective map

\varphi:{\mathscr{E}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{F}}
where
{\mathscr{E}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
.

Proof.
First, (
1
) is obvious. Now, (
2
) is a restatement of Corollary
2.8
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
So,
X\subseteq Y:=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open, for some
Y
.
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a complete intersection closed subset as in (

).
Let

. Then there is a resolution

(23)

where
n=\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}-k
.

. Then

. Therefore, assume

.

) there is a surjective map

, with

. Let

. So, we have the exact sequence

. Again, by
