3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{L}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.49498pt\raise 5.93056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{d_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.52783pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 111.52783pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{L}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 132.17903pt\raise 5.93056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{d_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 152.26398pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 152.26398pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 188.79178pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 188.79178pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad{\rm with}\qquad{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\in{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X).

Now the proof is complete, inductively.

Lemma 3.9
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
So,
X\subseteq Y:=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open for some
Y
.
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset and
grade(Z,X)=k
.
Consider an exact sequence

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.2778pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.2778pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.8056pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 103.8056pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.65283pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 141.65283pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad\qquad{\rm in}\qquad{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X).

1.

If
{\mathscr{K}},{\mathscr{G}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
then
{\mathscr{F}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
.

2.

If
{\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{G}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
then
{\mathscr{K}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
.

Proof.
Suppose
{\mathscr{K}},{\mathscr{G}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. It is immediate
{\mathscr{F}}\in{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
and
k=\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}({\mathscr{F}})
. So,
{\mathscr{F}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. This settles (
1
).

Now
{\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{G}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. So,
{\mathscr{K}}\in{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. It is also clear
\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{K}}}\leq k
.
Since
{\mathscr{K}}\in{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
,
grade({\mathscr{F}})\geq k
. So,
k\leq grade({\mathscr{F}})\leq\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}\leq k
.
So,
\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}=k
. This settles (
2
).
The proof is complete.

The following casts the above in the mold of resolving subcategories.

Corollary 3.10
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
), (
C.2
).
So,
X\subseteq Y:=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open.
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a complete intersection closed subset, as in (
2.1
).

Then
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\subseteq Coh^{Z}(X)
is a resolving subcategory (in the sense of
[
M15d
, Def 3.1]
).
Further,

{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)=\left\{{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh^{Z}(X):\dim_{C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)}{\mathscr{F}}<\infty\right\}

Proof.
Follows directly from (
3.10
,
3.7
).

Now, we are ready to state and prove the second equivalence theorem.

Theorem 3.11
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
So,
X\subseteq Y:=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open, for some
Y
.
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a complete intersection closed subset, as in (
2.1
).
Then the natural functor

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 33.46457pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-33.46457pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.37395pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.46457pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 57.46457pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad{\rm is~an~equivalence.}

(24)

Proof.
Consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon\mathfrak{q}}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{K}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{\quad{\rm in}\quad{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)}

(25)

Here the second line is a given exact sequence in
{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
, and
{\mathscr{F}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
.
By (
3.7
), there is a surjective map
\mathfrak{q}_{1}:{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
, with

{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. Let
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}=\ker(\varepsilon\mathfrak{q}_{1})
. Then
\mathfrak{q}_{1}
induces a
map
\mathfrak{q}_{2}:{\mathscr{K}}_{1}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
. In fact,
\mathfrak{q}_{2}
is surjective (use Snake Lemma). Further,

{\mathscr{K}}_{1}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
, by (
3.10
).

This shows that
[
K99
, Cond b, §1.5]
is satisfied by the functor

\zeta:C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. By (
3.10
), any object
{\mathscr{F}}\in{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
has a finite resolution by objects in
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. In other words,
[
K99
, Cond a, §1.5]

is also satisfied by the functor

\zeta
.

By
[
K99
, Last para of §1.5]
(the dual version), it follows that the map (
24
),
which is induced by
\zeta
, is an equivalence.
The proof is complete.

The following is the summary of the equivalences that we have established.

Theorem 3.12
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
So,
X\subseteq Y:=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
is open, for some
Y
.
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed complete intersection subset, as in (
2.1
).
Then we have the following
equivalences of Derived categories:

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\eta}

(26)

All the functor
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\eta
are defined naturally. The inverse of
\eta
is given by double complex trick. For future reference (
5.3
), we note that there is a natural equivalence

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{-1}\beta\alpha}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)}

(27)

An object
{\mathscr{F}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
is treated as a complex concentrated at degree zero. We have

\eta^{-1}\beta\alpha({\mathscr{F}})={\rm a~resolution~of}~{\mathscr{F}}

Proof.
This follows from Theorems
3.11
and
3.2
.
The proof is complete.

4
The
{\mathbb{K}}
-Theory Localization sequence

In this section we discuss
the main results on
{\mathbb{K}}
-Theory exact sequences, in this article. By

{\mathbb{K}}
-Theory, we mean non connective version of
K
-theory, which takes values in
the category
\mathrm{Sp}
of spectrum of pointed spaces
[
M23
, pp 402]
. We will not discuss connective
K
-theory. However, for idempotent complete exact categories
{\mathscr{E}}
, the nonnegative groups
K_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\cong{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)~\forall n\geq 0
are isomorphic
[
M23
, pp 406]
. Subsequently, for an exact category
{\mathscr{E}}
the
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra of
{\mathscr{E}}
will be denoted by

{\mathbb{K}}({\mathscr{E}})

[
S11
, Section 2.4.3]
. Unless stated otherwise, we talk about
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory of exact categories only.

Theorem 4.1
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a complete intersection closed subset, as in (
2.1
). and Write
U=X-Z
.
Then the sequence

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.93544pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}

(28)

is a homotopy fibratiion of spectra.
Consequently,
\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, there is a long exact sequence of
{\mathbb{K}}
-groups:

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n-1}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

(29)

We further have homotopy equivalences:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\\
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\\
\end{array}\right.

(30)

Proof.
As in (
C.1
), for any exact subcategory
{\mathscr{B}}(X)\subseteq Coh(X)
, denote the subcategories

Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{B}}(X)\right)=\left\{{\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\in Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{B}}(X)\right):{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{\bullet}\right)\in Coh^{Z}(X)~\forall~i\right\}

We use this notation for
{\mathscr{B}}(X)={\mathscr{V}}(X),~{\mathbb{M}}(X)
and others. These are treated as complicial exact categories with weak equivalences (quasi isomorphisms).
By Agreement theorem
[
S11
, 2.4.3, 3.2.30]
,
[
M23
, pp 409]
it follows

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}\left(X\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)\\
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}\left(U\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(U)\right)\right)\\
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)\\
{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)\\
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}\left(X\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\right)\\
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}\left(U\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\right)\\
\end{array}\right.\quad{\rm are~homotopy~equivalences~in}~\mathrm{Sp}.

Now consider the sequence of functors

\textstyle{Ch^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)}

of complicial exact categories. The functors of associated exact categories

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)}

is an equivalences, by Theorem
3.12
. By Triangular equivalence (
[
S11
, Thm 3.2.29]
,
[
M23
, pp 409]
), it follows that the following maps of
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 43.64471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-43.64471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.64471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.64471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 171.07166pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 171.07166pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad in\quad\mathrm{Sp}

are homotopy equivalences. Combining with the above, we have a sequence of homotopy equivalences

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 25.05975pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.05975pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.05975pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 49.05975pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 148.47003pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 148.47003pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad in\quad\mathrm{Sp}

of
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra.
Further,
the functor of complicial exact categories, with weak equivalences (i.e. the quasi isomorphisms)

Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\longrightarrow Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\qquad{\rm induce~the~functor}\quad\zeta:{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right).

The latter functor is also an equivalence, by Theorem
3.2
. Hence by the triangular equivalence theorem (
[
S11
, Thm 3.2.29]
,
[
M23
, pp 409]
), it follows
the map

{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}

is a homotopy equivalence. Putting all these together,

\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

is a zig-zag sequence of homotopy equivalences of spectra.
Of course, homotopy inverse of the last map is also given by a functor,
by double complex.

Now consider the sequences for functors

\textstyle{Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(U)\right)}

The induced diagram of the associated triangulated categories is as follows:

\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(U)\right)}

The lower line is exact up to factor (direct summand), by
[
S11
, Prop A.4.7]
. Since the vertical maps are equivalences, the first line is also exact up to factor.
Consider the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(U)\right)\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

of maps of spectra.
By Waldhausen-Thomason localization (
[
S11
, 3.2.27]
,
[
M23
, pp 408]
) it follows that the third line is
a homotopy fibration. Further the vertical maps are homotopy equivalences. Therefore, the sequence

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 27.0042pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.0042pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.0042pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 51.0042pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.90703pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 120.90703pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}

(31)

is a homotopy fibration.
By similar argument, using the Agreement theorem
[
S11
, 3.2.30]
and
[
S11
, Thm 3.2.29]
,
the following are equivalences of homotopy spectra:

\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}(X)\right)\right)}

\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(Ch^{b}\left({\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)}

This establishes the equivalences (
30
). Combining with (
31
) it follows that the sequence (
28
)
is also a homotopy fibration.
The proof is complete.

5
The
{\mathbb{G}}W
-Theory Localization sequence

In this section we develop exact sequence of
{\mathbb{G}}W
-groups, similar to (
28
,
29
). We refer to section
C
for notations, in particular
X
denotes a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian
affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
. We assume
1/2\in A
, in this section and whenever we talk about hermitian (
GW
) theory.
Further refer to sections
B
,
B.1
,
B.2
,
B.3
for background on
GW
theory, Karroubi

{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum
\mathrm{BiSp}
and others. In particular, we distinguish
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum
{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)^{\pm}\in\mathrm{BiSp}
of an exact category
{\mathscr{E}}
with duality and
the bispectrum
{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\in\mathrm{BiSp}
of the dg category

{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}
of its bounded chain complexes. We will only discuss Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispetrum, outside the background given in section
B
.

The

{\mathbb{G}}W
-theory differs from
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory, in that that, for quasi projective schemes
X
over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
, the category
{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
does not have a natural duality. So, to do
{\mathbb{G}}W
-theory (or
G{\mathcal{W}}
,
\mathfrak{g}{W}
theory), our only option remains is to work with the category

C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
of perfect modules, which has a natural duality. Because of this our results in the section are sharper than the results on
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory in section
4
.

Definition 5.1
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset and
grade(Z,X)=k
. Fix an invertible sheaf
{\mathscr{L}}
on
X
. Define the functor

^{\vee}:=\left(-\right)^{\vee}_{\mathscr{L}}:C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\quad{\rm by}\quad{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee}={\mathscr{E}}xt^{k}\left({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{L}}\right)

(32)

Usually, we ignore to mention the label
{\mathscr{L}}
. It is easy to check that
∨
defines a duality.

Following are some more notations
and some comments.

Notation 5.2
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Assume
1/2\in A
.
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset and
grade(Z,X)=k
. We also fix an invertible sheaf

{\mathscr{L}}
on
X
.

1.

Recall the notations
{\bf P}erf(X)
,
{\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)
,
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}(X)
and others from section
C
. We will be mindful to add prefix
{\bf dg}
to an exact category, when we want to distinguish these two.

2.

Given
X
, as above, we fix a minimal injective resolution
{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{I}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{I}}_{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{I}}_{-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

(33)

Clearly,
{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{P}}erf(X)
.

Consequently,
{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}:={\mathscr{L}}\otimes{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}\in{\mathscr{P}}erf(X)
, is an injective resolution of
{\mathscr{L}}
.

For
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in{\bf P}erf(X)
, we denote

{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee}:={\mathscr{F}}^{\vee}_{\mathscr{L}}={\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}\right).

(34)

3.

(
Lemma:
) It is a standard fact that the association
{\mathscr{F}}\mapsto{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee}

endows
\left({\bf dg}{\bf P}erf(X),\mathfrak{q}\right)
with a structure of a dg category with duality and weak equivalences.
Here
\mathfrak{q}
denotes the subcategory of quasi isomorphisms in
{\bf P}erf(X)
.
For a proof see
[
S17
, Lem 4.8]
.

Now we formulate a duality version of the main equivalence Theorem
3.12
, as follows.

Proposition 5.3
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
, with
1/2\in A
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a complete intersection closed subset, as in (
2.1
), and
grade(Z,X)=k
. We also fix an invertible sheaf

{\mathscr{L}}
on
X
.
Then there is a natural duality preserving equivalence

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)}

(35)

of derived categories. Here
T
denotes the shift functor, on either side. The duality on the left side is induced by the duality given in Definition
5.1
. The duality
\#
on right side is also induced by that on
{\mathscr{V}}(X)
, namely
\#:=T^{k}{\mathcal{H}}om\left(-,{\mathscr{L}}\right)
.

Proof.
As was commented in Theorem

3.12
, Equation
27
, there is a
natural equivalence

\omega:{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)

of derived categories, given by resolutions. For
{\mathscr{F}}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
, we have
\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}=k
.
It follows from this that
\omega
is actually an equivalence, as required (
35
).
The proof is complete.

Readers are referred to
[
S17
, §1.10, pp. 1747]
,
[
M23
, Def 10.2.4, 10.2.15]
for the jargon on dg categories with duality and weak equivalences. As usual, in chain complex categories, the quasi isomorphisms
\mathfrak{q}
are the weak equivalences, by default.

Lemma 5.4
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
with
1/2\in A
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset and
grade(Z,X)=k
. We also fix an invertible sheaf

{\mathscr{L}}
on
X
. Then

1.

The inclusion
\zeta:{\bf dg}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\longrightarrow{\bf dg}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)

is a duality preserving morphism of pointed dg categories with weak equivalences and duality. Here the duality on
{\mathscr{V}}(X)
is given by
{\mathscr{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{L}}\right)
, and duality on right hand side is as in (
34
).

Further, the functor

\mathfrak{T}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\right)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{T}\left({\bf dg}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\right)

of the associated triangulated categories, is a duality preserving equivalence.

2.

The inclusion
\beta:{\bf dg}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\longrightarrow T^{k}\left({\bf dg}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\right)

is a duality preserving morphism, of pointed dg categories with weak equivalences and duality, where
T
denotes the shift. The duality on
{\bf dg}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
is induced by (
32
).

Further, assume that
Z
is complete intersection, as in (
2.1
). Then the functor

\mathfrak{T}\left({\bf dg}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)\longrightarrow T^{k}\mathfrak{T}\left({\bf dg}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\right)

of the associated triangulated categories is a duality preserving equivalence.

Proof.
First, for
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in Ch^{b}({\mathscr{V}})
the map

{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{L}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}\right)

is a quasi isomorphism. By definition,
\zeta
preserves duality.
Now,
we have the associated triangulated categories

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathfrak{T}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\right)={\mathscr{D}}_{Z}^{b}({\mathscr{V}}(X)\\
\mathfrak{T}\left({\bf dg}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\right)={\mathscr{D}}_{Z}\left({\bf P}erf(X)\right)\\
\end{array}\right.

In fact, the natural inclusion functor
{\bf D}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\bf D}\left({\bf P}erf(X)\right)
is an equivalence, by
[
TT90
, Lem 3.8]
,
[
S11
, Prop 3.4.8]
.
Restricting this equivalence, to the complexes with support on
Z
, we obtain that

{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}_{Z}\left({\bf P}erf(X)\right)

is an equivalence. This completes the proof of (
1
).

Now we proceed to prove (
2
). We use the generic notation
∨
to denote the dualities,
for all categories. First, we prove that
\beta
preserves duality.
For
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in Ch^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
, its dual is given as follows:

{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}^{\vee}:\quad\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 202.4772pt\raise-32.21722pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 295.12555pt\raise-32.21722pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 295.12555pt\raise-32.21722pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}

We are required to prove that

{\mathscr{F}}^{\vee}_{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}\right){\rm is~a~quasi~isomorphism}

Since
{\mathscr{F}}_{i}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\subseteq C{\mathbb{M}}^{k}(X)
, we have
k=grade\left({\mathscr{F}}_{i}\right)=\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}_{i}}
. Therefore

{\mathscr{E}}xt^{r}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{i},{\mathscr{L}}\right)=0\qquad\forall i\quad{\rm and}\quad r\neq k

With this summary, for
{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in Ch^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)
, we obtain the double complex:

\textstyle{\deg=-n\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}xt^{k}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n-1},{\mathscr{L}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}xt^{k}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n},{\mathscr{L}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}xt^{k}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n+1},{\mathscr{L}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n-1},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-k}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-k}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n+1},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-k}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n-1},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-(k+1)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-(k+1)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n+1},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-(k+1)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n-1},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-(k+2)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-(k+2)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{n+1},{\mathscr{L}}{\mathscr{I}}_{-(k+2)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\cdots}

Again, since
{\mathscr{F}}_{n}\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
and
k=grade(Z,X)
, all the vertical lines above are exact.
From this double complex, we obtain a natural transformation:

\textstyle{\beta\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}^{\vee}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\beta\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)^{\vee}=:{\mathcal{H}}om\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet},{\mathscr{I}}_{\bullet}\right)}

which is a quasi isomorphism. This establishes that
\beta
preserves duality.
Further, the associated triangulated categories

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathfrak{T}\left({\bf dg}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)={\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\\
\mathfrak{T}\left({\bf dg}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\right)=T^{k}{\bf D}_{Z}\left({\bf P}erf(X)\right)\\
\end{array}\right.

Now we have a commutative diagram of functors:

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{T^{k}{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{T^{k}{\bf D}_{Z}\left({\bf P}erf(X)\right)}

When
Z
is complete intersection the horizontal arrow is an equivalence by (
3.12
). The vertical arrow is also an equivalence by (
1
).
This completes the proof of (
2
).

5.1
Homotopy fibrations of
{\mathbb{G}}W
Theory bi-spectra
{\mathrm{BiSp}}

Now we state the equivalence theorem of
{\mathbb{G}}W
-theory bi-Spectra.
Refer to section
B
,
C
for notations and definitions on
GW
-theory, spectra and bispectra.
For a dg category
{\mathscr{A}}=\left({\mathscr{A}},\mathfrak{q},^{\vee},\varpi\right)
with duality and weak equivalences, the
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum

{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{A}}\right)\in\mathrm{BiSp}
takes values in
the category
\mathrm{BiSp}
of bi-spectra (see §
B.3
).
The
{\mathbb{G}}W
-groups

{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)
are defined as the homotopy groups
\forall n,p\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Similar notations are used for exact categories with duality and weak equivalences
(see §
B
).

Theorem 5.5
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a complete intersection closed subset, as in (
2.1
),
and
grade(Z,X)=k
.
We also fix an invertible sheaf

{\mathscr{L}}
on
X
. We assume
1/2\in A
. Then the following zig-zag sequence

\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim\hskip 16.38895pt}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r+k]}\left({\bf dg}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r+k]}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 16.38895pt\sim}

are stable homotopy equivalences
in the category
\mathrm{BiSp}
of bispectra,
\forall r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Consequently, the
{\mathbb{G}}W
-theory groups

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r]}_{p}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\cong{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}^{[r+k]}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\quad{\rm are~isomorphic}~\forall~r,p\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

Proof.
Follows directly from Theorem
5.4
, and
[
S17
, Thm 8.9]
,

[
M23
, pp 466]
. The proof is complete.

Now we are ready to state the theorem on homotopy fibration of
{\mathbb{G}}W
-bispectra.

Theorem 5.6
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed complete intersection subset, as in (
2.1
), with
grade(Z,X)=k
and let
U=X-Z
. We also fix an invertible sheaf

{\mathscr{L}}
on
X
. We assume
1/2\in A
.
Then the sequence

\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r+k]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r+k]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}

(36)

is a homotopy fibration in the category
\mathrm{BiSp}
of bispectra
\forall r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
The first map here is the zig-zag map given in (
5.5
).
Consequently, there is a long exact sequence

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}^{[r]}\left({\bf dg}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}^{[r+k]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}^{[r+k]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{p-1}^{[r]}\left({\bf dg}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\forall p,r\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

(37)

Proof.
Consider the sequence of dg categories with dualities and weak equivalences (quasi isomorphism)

\textstyle{{\bf dg}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{k}{\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{k}{\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U)}

The corresponding sequence of associated triangulated categories is as follows:

\textstyle{{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{k}{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{k}{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}

This sequence is exact up to factor (direct summand), by
[
S11
, Prop A.4.7]
,
because
{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}({\mathscr{V}}(X))
is an equivalence by
(
3.12
). Now the proof follows from

[
S17
, Theorem 8.10]
that the sequence (
36
) is a homotopy fibration
in
\mathrm{BiSp}
.
The proof is complete.

Remark 5.7
.

Let
A
be a commutative ring and
{\mathscr{E}}:=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)
be a
A
-linear exact category with duality. Assume
1/2\in A
. For the purpose of making statements on
{\mathbb{G}}W
-bispectrum of
{\mathscr{E}}
, we
defined
{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})\in\mathrm{BiSp}
directly, in section
B
, without any reference to the definition of

{\mathbb{G}}W({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}})\in\mathrm{BiSp}
as a dg category. Our sense of esthetics and completeness of the theory demands this. Refer to section
B
for further discussions. We point out that two
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectra

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right):={\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)^{+}:={\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\\
{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)^{-}:={\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},-\varpi\right)\\
\end{array}\right.\qquad\in{\mathrm{BiSp}}

are defined, while

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\in\mathrm{BiSp}
has four periodicity.

Now, we interpret Theorem
5.5
the above in terms of
{\mathbb{G}}W
-bispetrum of exact categories.

Theorem 5.8
.

Suppose
X
is a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Use other notations, as in (
C.1
,
C.2
).
Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset, and
grade(Z,X)=k
. We also fix an invertible sheaf

{\mathscr{L}}
on
X
. We assume
1/2\in A
. Then the following

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\cong{\mathbb{G}}W^{[k]}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\\
{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)^{-}\cong{\mathbb{G}}W^{[k+2]}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\\
\end{array}\right.\quad{\rm are~stable~equivalences~in}~\mathrm{BiSp}

of zig-zag maps.

Proof.
Follows from (
5.5
), with
r=0,2
. The proof is complete.

The following is reinterpretation of (
5.6
), for even shifts.

X

.

),

. Write

Then the sequences

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 36.38683pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-36.38683pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.38683pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.38683pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.19243pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 141.19243pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}&in~case~k=0~mod~4\\

(38)

are homotopy fibratiion in the category
\mathrm{BiSp}

{\mathbb{G}}W
groups, corresponding to (
38
) can be written down.

Proof.

5.6
), by taking

. See Definition Remark

and equation (

). The proof is complete.

6
Applications

Localization theorems, such as Theorems
4.1
,
5.5
,

5.9
, usually would have some consequences. In this section, we list some of them.
Usually, we consider only idempotent complete the exact categories
{\mathscr{E}}
and exact categories with duality

\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)
. For such idempotent complete exact categories
{\mathscr{E}}
, we use the notations:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathbb{K}}_{i}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)=:{K}_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)&\forall i\geq 0\\
{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}_{i}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}},\mathfrak{q},^{\vee},\varpi\right)=:{G}W^{[n]}_{i}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee},\varpi\right)&\forall i\geq 0,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\\
\end{array}\right.

where
\mathfrak{q}
denotes the subcategory of quasi isomorphisms.
First, we state the application to the punctured schemes.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
(A,\mathfrak{m})
be a Cohen Macaulay local ring and
X=\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
,

Z=V(\mathfrak{m})
and
U=X-Z
. Assume
1/2\in A
and write
d=\dim A
.
Then the sequence

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.93544pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}

is a homotopy fibration. Consequently, there is a long exact sequence of
{\mathbb{K}}
-groups.
Further,

{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}K_{r}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)&\forall r\geq 0\\
0&otherwise\\
\end{array}\right.

Consequently,

\forall r\leq-1\quad{\rm the~map}\quad{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}(X)}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}(U)}\right)\quad{\rm is~an~isomorphism.}

In particular, if
U
is regular (i.e.
A
is regular in codimension
d-1
) then

{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}(X)}\right)=0\qquad\qquad\forall r\leq-1

Proof.
Let
d=\dim(A)
. Note
Z=V(\mathfrak{f}_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
is complete intersection. Now, the first statement is an immediate consequence of the localization theorem
4.1
of
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra.
Note,
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
represents the
category of
A
-modules, with finite length and finite projective dimension.
Further note that

C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
is a noetherian abelian category. Now it follows from
[
S06
, Thm 7]
that

\forall r\leq-1\quad{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)=0

Now, it follows from the long exact sequence that
{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\cong{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)~\forall r\leq-1
. If
U
is regular then

{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)=0~\forall r\leq-1

[
S11
, pp 182]
. So, the last statement follows.
The proof is complete.

The above (
6.1
) can be improved, as follows.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Let
Z=\left\{\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\right\}\subseteq X
be finite set of closed points.
Further, assume that
Z
is complete intersection (
2.1
) and
U=X-Z
.
Then the sequence

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.93544pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}

is a homotopy fibration. Consequently, there is a long exact sequence of
{\mathbb{K}}
-groups.
Further,

{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}K_{r}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{V(\mathfrak{m}_{i})}\left(\mathrm{Spec}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{X,\mathfrak{m}_{i}}\right)\right)\right)&\forall r\geq 0\\
0&otherwise\\
\end{array}\right.

(39)

Consequently,

\forall r\leq-1\quad{\rm the~map}\quad{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}(X)}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}(U)}\right)\quad{\rm is~an~isomorphism.}

In particular, if
U
is regular, then

{\mathbb{K}}_{r}\left({\mathscr{V}(X)}\right)=0\qquad\qquad\forall r\leq-1

Proof.
Note that
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
is a noetherian abelian category. Now, there is an equivalence
\Psi:C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\prod_{i}C{\mathbb{M}}^{V(\mathfrak{m}_{i})}(X)
.
To define
\Psi
let
M\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
. For
i=1,\ldots,\ell
define
M_{i}\in{\mathbb{M}}^{V(\mathfrak{m}_{i})}(X)
, as follows

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(M_{i})_{|X-\{\mathfrak{m}_{1},\ldots\hat{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}\ldots,\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\}}=M_{|X-\{\mathfrak{m}_{1},\ldots\hat{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}\ldots,\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\}}\\
(M_{i})_{|X-\{\mathfrak{m}_{i}\}}=0\\
\end{array}\right.\quad Define\quad\Psi(M)=\left(M_{1},\ldots,M_{\ell}\right)

(40)

The inverse functor is defined by direct sum.
Finally, there are equivalences

\Phi:C{\mathbb{M}}^{V(\mathfrak{m}_{i})}\left(X\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}C{\mathbb{M}}^{V(\mathfrak{m}_{i})}\left(\mathrm{Spec}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{X,\mathfrak{m}_{i}}\right)\right)\quad M\mapsto M_{\mathfrak{m}_{i}}

(41)

Now the proof is completed, as in the proof of (
6.1
).
The proof is complete.

Remark 6.3
.

If
\mathfrak{m}:=\mathfrak{m}_{i}
is a regular point, then the equation can be further simplified, as follows. Suppose

(A,\mathfrak{m},\kappa)
is a regular local ring. Then by Dévissage theorem

[
M23
, pp 111]

{\mathbb{K}}_{r}C{\mathbb{M}}^{V(\mathfrak{m})}\left(\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}K_{r}\left(\kappa\right)&r\geq 0\\
0&r\leq-1\\
\end{array}\right.

We leave it to the reader to write
a
{\mathbb{G}}W
-version of (
6.1
). However, negative
{\mathbb{G}}W
groups noetherian abelian categories do not necessarily vanish, as in
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory.
We write down the
{\mathbb{G}}W
-version of (
6.2
), as follows.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
,
with
1/2\in A
.
Let
Z=\left\{\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\right\}\subseteq X
be finite set of closed points.
Further, assume that
Z
is complete intersection (
2.1
), with

d=grade(Z,X)
and
U=X-Z
.
Then
\forall r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, the sequence

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 46.93427pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-46.93427pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 70.93427pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 70.93427pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r+d]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 183.13654pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 183.13654pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r+d]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}~~in~~\mathrm{BiSp}

(42)

is a homotopy fibration in
\mathrm{BiSp}
. Further,

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\prod_{i=0}^{\ell}{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{V(\mathfrak{m}_{i})}\left(X_{\mathfrak{m}_{i}}\right)\right)

(43)

is a stable equivalence in
\mathrm{BiSp}
, where
X_{\mathfrak{m}_{i}}=\mathrm{Spec}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{X,\mathfrak{m}_{i}}\right)
.

Consequently, the sequence (
42
), leads to long exact sequences of

{\mathbb{G}}W
-groups.
Further, one can reinterpret (
42
) in terms of
{\mathbb{G}}W
-spectra of the exact categories, as in (
38
), which we skip.

Proof.
Same as the proof of (
6.2
), while we use the theorem
5.6
.
Also note that the functors
\Psi
and
\Phi
(
40
,
41
)
are duality preserving equivalences.
The proof is complete.

When the points
\mathfrak{m}_{i}
in (
6.4
) are regular, the Dévissage theorems
for

{\mathbb{G}}W
-groups or bispectrum applies
[
M24
]
. Thus the groups

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[r]}_{n}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{V(\mathfrak{m}_{i})}\left(\mathrm{Spec}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{X,\mathfrak{m}_{i}}\right)\right)\right)
have better descriptions, as follows.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
\left(R,\mathfrak{m},\kappa\right)
be a noetherian regular local ring,
X=\mathrm{Spec}\left(R\right)
,
Z=V(\mathfrak{m})
,
d=\dim R
. Note

C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)
is the category of
R
-modules
M
with finite length
(
and
\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{M}=d
). Then

C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)
has a natural duality
M^{\vee}=Ext^{d}\left(M,R\right)
.
Let
\varpi
denote the double dual identification in
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)
.
As usual,
\mathscr{V}(\kappa)
denotes the category of finite dimensional
\kappa
-vector spaces. Note
\mathscr{V}(\kappa)
also has a natural duality, defined by
M^{\vee}=Hom\left(M,\kappa\right)
. Let
\mathfrak{e}
denote the double dual identification in
{\mathscr{V}}(\kappa)
,
given by evaluation. Then there is a natural equivalence, of bi spectra:

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),\mathfrak{q},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\cong{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(\kappa),\mathfrak{q},^{\vee},\mathfrak{e}\right)\qquad\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

Here, on left side,
\varpi
continues to denote the double dual identification induced by
\varpi
, and
\mathfrak{q}
denotes quasi isomorphism.
We write

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}W(\kappa)=W\left({\mathscr{V}}(\kappa),^{\vee},\mathfrak{e}\right)&W(\kappa)^{-}=W\left({\mathscr{V}}(\kappa),^{\vee},-\mathfrak{e}\right)\\
GW_{p}(\kappa)=GW_{p}\left({\mathscr{V}}(\kappa),^{\vee},\mathfrak{e}\right)&GW_{p}(\kappa)^{-}=GW\left({\mathscr{V}}(\kappa),^{\vee},-\mathfrak{e}\right)\\
\end{array}\right.

Consequently,

{\mathbb{G}}{W}^{[n]}_{p}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),^{\vee},\mathfrak{q},\varpi\right)\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}W(\kappa)&p\leq-1,n-p-d=0~mod~4\\
0&p\leq-1,~n-p-d=1,2,3~mod~4\\
G{W}_{p}(\kappa)&n=0~mod~4,~p\geq 0\\
G{W}_{p}(\kappa)^{-}&n=2~mod~4,p\geq 0\\
\end{array}\right.

For
p\geq 0
and
n=2m+1
odd, we have an exact sequence (see
[
M24
]
).

Proof.
See
[
M24
]
.

The following is an immediate corollary.

Corollary 6.6
.

Consider the setup and hypotheses of (
6.5
). Then

{\mathbb{G}}{W}_{p}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),^{\vee},\varpi\right)\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}W(\kappa)&p\leq-1,p+d=0~mod~4\\
0&p\leq-1,~p+d=1,2,3~mod~4\\
GW_{p}(\kappa)&p\geq 0\\
\end{array}\right.

and

{\mathbb{G}}{W}_{p}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),^{\vee},\varpi\right)^{-}\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}W(\kappa)&p\leq-1,p+d=2~mod~4\\
0&p\leq-1,~p+d=1,2,3~mod~4\\
G{W}_{p}(\kappa)^{-}&p\geq 0\\
\end{array}\right.

Proof.
Follows from (
6.5
) and section
B
.

The following is another example exploiting regularity.

Example 6.8
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Let
U\subseteq X
be an open subset of
X
, and
Z=X-U
be complete intersection,
as in (
2.1
). Assume that
U
is regular. Then

{\mathbb{K}}_{p}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\cong{\mathbb{K}}_{p}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\qquad\forall p\leq-2

and the long exact sequence on the left terminates at the term
{\mathbb{K}}_{-1}({\mathscr{V}}(X))
.

Proof.
By the localization theorem
4.1
, the sequence

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.93544pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}

is a homotopy fibration, of
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectra.
By

[
S06
, Thm 7]
,
we have
{\mathbb{K}}_{k}({\mathscr{V}}(U))={\mathbb{K}}_{k}(Coh(U))=0~\forall k\leq-1
. The rest follows from the long exact sequence.

We use Theorem
5.9
to obtain a
{\mathbb{G}}W
analogue of
example
6.9
.

Example 6.9
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
Let
U\subseteq X
be an open subset of
X
, and
Z=X-U
be complete intersection,
as in (
2.1
). Assume that
U
is regular and
grade(Z,X)=k
. Also, let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a fixed line bundle on
X
and
1/2\in A
. Then
corresponding to each of the four homotopy fibrations in (
38
), we obtain an exact sequence of
{\mathbb{G}}W
groups. We only write down the exact sequence corresponding to the first homotopy fibration in (
38
),
which applies when
k=0~mod~4
, as follows:

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf GW}_{0}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf GW}_{0}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bf GW}_{0}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{-1}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{-1}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W^{1}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{-2}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{-2}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)^{-}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{-3}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{-3}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W^{3}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{-4}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W_{-4}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}
\textstyle{\cdots}

Proof.
We consider the case
k=4k_{0}
only. We start with the homotopy fibration

\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}

All the non negative groups are the homotopy groups of the
GW
-spaces
[
M23
, 450, 466]
. Note
{\mathbb{K}}_{p}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)=0~\forall p\leq-1
. It follows from this that

[
S17
, pp 1805]

\mathfrak{G}{W}_{p}({\mathscr{V}}(U))\cong G{\mathcal{W}}_{p}({\mathscr{V}}(U))\cong{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}({\mathscr{V}}(U))~\qquad\qquad\forall p\leq-1

Therefore

\forall p\leq-1
we have

{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}({\mathscr{V}}(U))\cong{G}{\mathcal{W}}_{p}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U))\cong W^{-p}({\mathscr{V}}(U))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}W({\mathscr{V}}(U))&if~p=0~mod~4\\
W({\mathscr{V}}(U))^{-}&if~p=2~mod~4\\
\end{array}\right.

The proof is complete.

Thomason
[
TT90
]
considered the
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory of perfect complexes, and likewise Schlichting
[
S11
,
S17
]
considered
{\mathbb{G}}W
-theory of perfect complexes. We differ from
[
TT90
,
S11
,
S17
]
in that that we grind down the theory to the level of exact subcategories
of
Coh(X)
(see Example
1.3
). For notations
{\bf P}erf(X)
and
{\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)
refer to (
C.1
). Notationally, we denote Thomason
{\mathbb{K}}
theory by
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf(X)\right)
and likewise, to distinguish them from our theory on exact categories.

Theorem 6.10

(Agreement)
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
,
U\subseteq X
be open and
Z=X-U
. Assume that
Z
is complete intersection, as in
(
2.1
).
Then the natural degree zero inclusion
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\longrightarrow{\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)
induces, a homotopy equivalence:

{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}.

Proof.
We have commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}\left({\bf P}erf(U)\right)}

of Homotopy fibration. For the lower fibration see
[
TT90
,
S11
]
or
[
M23
, pp 411]
, and the upper fibration is an application of (
4.1
). It is known that the
2^{nd}
and
3^{rd}
vertical maps are homotopy equivalence (see
[
TT90
,
S11
]
or
[
M23
, pp 410]
. Now one can write down the commutative diagram of the homotopy groups and use Five Lemma. The proof is complete.

Schlichting
[
S17
, pp 1804]
considers
{\mathbb{G}}W
-theory of
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X)
(
denoted by

sPerf(X)
in
[
S17
]
). We have a
{\mathbb{G}}W
-bispectra analog to (
6.10
).

Theorem 6.11

(Agreement)
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
,
U\subseteq X
be open and
Z=X-U
. Assume
1/2\in A
and
Z
is complete intersection, as in
(
2.1
), with
k=grade(Z,X)
. Let
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and
{\mathcal{L}}
be a line bundle on
X
. Then the natural map

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n-k]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),\mathfrak{q},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}{\mathscr{V}}(X),\mathfrak{q},^{\vee{\mathcal{L}}},\varpi\right)\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{BiSp}

is a stable homotopy equivalence in the category
\mathrm{BiSp}
of bispectra. Here, on

{\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
, the duality
∨
is induced by
M\mapsto{\mathcal{E}}xt^{k}(M,{\mathcal{L}})
and on
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X)
the duality is induced by
P\mapsto{\mathcal{H}}om(M,{\mathcal{L}})
. Further, on both sides,
\varpi
denotes the double dual identifications, and
\mathfrak{q}
denotes quasi isomorphisms.
Further, there are equivalences in
\mathrm{BiSp}
, as follows

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),^{\vee},\varpi\right)&in~case~n-k=0~mod~4\\
{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),^{\vee},\varpi\right)^{-}&in~case~n-k=2~mod~4\\
\end{array}\right.\qquad\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}

Here on right side, the duality on the exact categories is given by
M\mapsto{\mathscr{E}}xt^{k}\left(M,{\mathcal{L}}\right)
.

Proof.
The latter part follows from former, using discussion in section
B
.
To prove the first part, consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n-k]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}_{Z}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U)\right)}

of Homotopy fibration in
\mathrm{BiSp}
.
The top left map here is the zig-zag map given in (
5.5
).
The upper fibration follows from (
5.5
), and the lower fibration follows from
[
S17
, pp 1807]
. The
2^{nd}
and
3^{rd}
vertical maps are identity. Hence the first vertical map is a homotopy equivalence in
\mathrm{BiSp}
. The proof is complete.

The Excision and Mayer-Vietories theorems would be routine consequence, as follows.

Theorem 6.12

(Excision)
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
. Let
V\subseteq X
be an open subset, and
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset such that

Z\subseteq V
. Assume that
Z
is complete intersection, as in (
2.1
).
Then the restriction map

{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{K}}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V)\right)\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}

is a homotopy equivalence of spectra. Consequently, the induced maps

{\mathbb{K}}_{p}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{K}}_{p}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V)\right)\quad{\rm are~isomorphisms}~\forall p\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

Proof.
One can use the Agreement theorem
6.10
, and refer to the Excision theorem
[
S11
, Thm 3.4.10]
. For a more direct proof, note that
by agreement and equivalence theorems
[
M23
, pp 409]
, we are reduced to proving that
the restriction functor of derived categories
{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X))\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V))\right)
is an equivalence. However, the functor

C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\longrightarrow C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V)\quad{\rm is~an~equivalence,in~fact,~is~a~bijection}.

The inverse functor is given by extending
M\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V)
by zero, on
X-Z
.
The proof is complete.

Same proof leads to a
{\mathbb{G}}W
-analogue of (
6.12
).

Theorem 6.13

(Excision)
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
. Let
V\subseteq X
be an open subset, and
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset such the

Z\subseteq V
. Assume
1/2\in A
and
{\mathcal{L}}
is a line bundle on
X
.
Further assume that
Z
is complete intersection on
X
, as in (
2.1
).
Then the restriction map

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),{\mathcal{L}}\right)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V),{\mathcal{L}}_{|V}\right)\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{BiSp}

is a homotopy equivalence in the category
\mathrm{BiSp}
of bispectra. Consequently, the induced maps

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}_{r}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),{\mathcal{L}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}_{r}\left({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V),{\mathcal{L}}_{|V}\right)\quad{\rm are~isomorphisms}~\forall n,r\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

Here on either side, with
k=grade(Z,X)
, the duality is given by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M^{\vee}={\mathscr{E}}xt^{k}\left(M,{\mathcal{L}}\right)&M\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\\
M^{\vee}={\mathscr{E}}xt^{k}\left(M,{\mathcal{L}}_{|V}\right)&M\in C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V)\\
\end{array}\right.

Proof.
Again the map
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)\longrightarrow C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(V\right)
is an equivalence
(In fact, a bijection). Hence the it induced functor
{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(X\right)\right)\longrightarrow{\bf D}^{b}\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}\left(V\right)\right)
is a duality preserving equivalence. Therefore, the proof is complete by the Invariance theorem
[
S17
, pp 1800]
,
[
M23
, pp 466]
.

The following are exact category version of the above (
6.13
).

Corollary 6.14

(Excision)
.

Consider the setup and the hypotheses of (
6.13
), then the following maps

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V)\right)\\
{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)\right)^{-}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{G}}W\left(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V)\right)^{-}\\
\end{array}\right.\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{BiSp}

are homotopy equivalences in
\mathrm{BiSp}
.

Proof.
Immediate from (
6.13
), by taking
n=0,2
. The proof is complete.

The Mayer-Vietories Theorem is, in fact, a routine consequence of excision theorem. So, as a consequence of (
6.12
), we obtain the following.

Theorem 6.15

(Mayer-Vietoris)
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
, and let
X=U\cup V
be an open cover. Assume
Z=X-U
is complete intersection in
X
,
as in (
2.1
).
Then the diagram

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 22.95142pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.95142pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}({\mathscr{V}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 56.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}({\mathscr{V}}(U))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.91043pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-22.44449pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}({\mathscr{V}}(V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.95142pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.95142pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
