is~a~cartesian~square~in~the~category}~\mathrm{Sp}~{\rm of~spectra}.

Consequently, there is a long exact sequence

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(U))\bigoplus{\mathbb{K}}_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(U\cap V))}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n-1}({\mathscr{V}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

of
{\mathbb{K}}
-groups.

Proof.
Write
Z=X-U=V-U\cap V\subseteq V
. Consider the diagram

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.93544pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\wr}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.93544pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}({\mathscr{V}}(V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 124.83827pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}({\mathscr{V}}(U\cap V))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{Sp}.

Both the horizontal lines are homotopy fibrations in
\mathrm{Sp}
, by Theorem
4.1
. The
1^{st}
vertical map is an isomorphism in
\mathrm{Sp}
, by Excision theorem
6.12
. This completes the proof that the given square is cartesian.
The long exact sequence above is established routinely, by comparing two exact sequences:

\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(U))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n-1}(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(U\cap V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{K}}_{n-1}(C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V))}

The proof is complete.

The same proof of (
6.16
) gives a Mayer-Vietories theorem for
{\mathbb{G}}W
theory, as follows.

Theorem 6.16

(Mayer-Vietoris)
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
, and let
X=U\cup V
be an open cover. Assume
1/2\in A
and
{\mathcal{L}}
be a line bundle on
X
. Assume
Z=X-U
is complete intersection in
X
,
as in (
2.1
). Then the diagram

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 48.00134pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-43.97168pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X),{\mathcal{L}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.50131pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 87.50131pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U),{\mathcal{L}}_{|U})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 135.4339pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise dg}{\mathscr{V}}(V),{\mathcal{L}}_{|U})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.00134pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U\cap V),{\mathcal{L}}_{|U\cap V})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad in\qquad\mathrm{BiSp}

is a cartesian square in the category
\mathrm{BiSp}
of bispectra
\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Consequently, there are long exact sequences
\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
of
{\mathbb{G}}W
-groups
(we ignore to include the
{\mathcal{L}}
-coordinate):

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}_{p}({\mathscr{V}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}_{p}({\mathscr{V}}(U))\bigoplus{\mathbb{G}}W_{n}({\mathscr{V}}(V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}_{p}({\mathscr{V}}(U\cap V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}_{p-1}({\mathscr{V}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

Proof.
Write
Z=X-U=V-U\cap V\subseteq V
, and
k=grade(Z,X)
. Consider the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(V))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{V}}(U\cap V))}

Both the horizontal lines are homotopy fibrations in
\mathrm{BiSp}
, by Theorem
5.5
. The
1^{st}
vertical map is an isomorphism in
\mathrm{BiSp}
, by Excision theorem
6.13
. This completes the proof that the given square is cartesian. Again, the exact sequences are established by routine methods. The proof is complete.

The following is the exact category version of (
6.16
).

(Mayer-Vietoris)
.

).

Appendix A
Grade and annihilator of coherent modules

While grade of coherent modules over affine schemes are fairly standard,
in this section, we discuss grade of coherent modules over quasi projective schemes.
First, we recall the definition of grade and summarize the basics on the same from
[
M70
, pp. 103]
.

Definition A.1
.

Suppose
A
is a noetherian ring,
X=\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
and
M\in Coh(A)
.

1.

Define

grade(M)=\inf\left\{r:Ext^{r}(M,A)\neq 0\right\}

Consequently, for an ideal
I
, define

grade\left(\frac{A}{I}\right)=\inf\left\{r:Ext^{r}\left(\frac{A}{I},A\right)\neq 0\right\}

(44)

Sometimes, one writes

grade(I):=grade\left(\frac{A}{I}\right)

This is not to be confused with the grade of
I
, as an
A
-module, which would be zero if
I
contains a non zero divisor.

2.

It follows, for
M\in Coh(A)
and
I=Ann(M)
, we have

grade(M)=depth_{I}(A)=grade\left(\frac{A}{I}\right)

(45)

3.

It is obvious from definition

grade(M)\leq\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}(M)

Corollary A.2
.

For an ideal
I
of
A
, we have

grade(I)=grade\left(\sqrt{I}\right)

Proof.
Let
r=grade(I)
and
s=grade\left(\sqrt{I}\right)
.
First,
r=depth_{I}(A)\leq depth_{\sqrt{I}}(A)=s
.
If
a_{1},\ldots a_{s}\in\sqrt{I}
is a regular sequence then so
is
a_{1}^{m},\ldots a_{s}^{m}\in I
for
m\gg 0
,
[
M70
, Thm 26, pp. 96]
. So,
s\leq r
. So,
s=r
.
The proof is complete.

We define grade of a closed subset of
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.

Definition A.3
.

Let
A
be a noetherian ring, and
X=\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
Let
Z=V(I)\subseteq X
be a closed subset. Define

grade(Z,X)=grade(I)=grade\left(\frac{A}{I}\right)

By (
A.2
), it is well defined and is independent of the choice of
I
.

We record the following non local version of
[
M70
, pp. 108, Thm 31]
.

Lemma A.4

(pp. 108, Thm 31
[
M70
]
)
.

Suppose
A
is a Cohen Macaulay ring (non local) and
M\in Coh(A)
. Then

grade(M)=height(I)\qquad{\rm where}\qquad I=Ann(M).

Subsequently, we wish to make some sense out of the idea of annihilators of coherent sheaves.

Definition A.5
.

Let
X
be a noetherian scheme and
{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(X)
.
Define the annihilator

ann({\mathscr{F}})(U)=\left\{t\in\Gamma\left(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right):t\cdot\Gamma\left(U,{\mathscr{F}}\right)=0\right\}\subseteq\Gamma\left(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right)

The definition makes sense.
However,
ann({\mathscr{F}})
does not seem to make sense, as a (pre)sheaf of ideals.
This is because the restriction maps

\Gamma\left(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right)\longrightarrow\Gamma\left(V,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right)

may not give a restriction map on
ann\left({\mathscr{F}}\right)
.

It is a standard fact that it makes sense when
X=\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
is affine.
Consequently, in a noetherian scheme
X
, on the affine pieces,

U_{i}=\mathrm{Spec}\left(A_{i}\right)=\mathrm{Spec}\left(\Gamma\left(U_{i},{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right)\right)
and

M_{i}=\Gamma\left(U_{i},{\mathscr{F}}\right)
the annihilators

I_{i}=ann(M_{i})
make sense.

Subsequently, we prove that
ann({\mathscr{F}})
makes sense when
X
is quasi projective.

A.1
(Quasi) projective schemes

Lemma A.6
.

Suppose
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
with
S=A[x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
and
\deg(x_{i})=1
. Assume
A
is noetherian.
Let
{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(Y)
and
{\mathscr{F}}=\widetilde{M}

for some finitely generated graded
S
-module
M
.
(
Refer to
[
H77
, §II.5, pp116]
for this
\widetilde{M}
construction
.)
Let
I=ann(M)
. Then
\widetilde{I}=ann({\mathscr{F}})
.

Proof.
First we need to prove that
ann({\mathscr{F}})
is a sheaf. From the affine case it is clear that
ann({\mathscr{F}})_{|D(g)}=\widetilde{ann\left(M_{(g)}\right)}~\forall~g
homogeneous. Now suppose
V\subseteq U
.
We need to give a map

ann\left({\mathscr{F}}_{|U}\right)\longrightarrow ann\left({\mathscr{F}}_{|V}\right)

First assume
V=\mathrm{Spec}\left(S_{(f)}\right)
, for some homogeneous
f\in S
.
Write
U=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Spec}\left(S_{(g_{i})}\right)
. There are maps

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}S_{(g_{i})}\longrightarrow S_{(fg_{i})}\\
M_{(g_{i})}\longrightarrow M_{(fg_{i})}\\
\end{array}\right.\Longrightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}ann\left(M_{(g_{i})}\right)\longrightarrow ann\left(M_{(fg_{i})}\right)\\
{\rm sending}\quad\frac{a}{g_{i}^{k}}\mapsto\frac{a}{g_{i}^{k}}\quad with\quad\deg(a)=\deg(g_{i}^{k})\\
\end{array}\right.

In deed, the commuting square

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 27.96518pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.96518pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{ann\left(M_{(g_{i})}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.96518pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-9.05556pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.33334pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 51.96518pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{ann\left(M_{(fg_{i})}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.2724pt\raise-9.05556pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.2724pt\raise-24.33334pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.38931pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S_{(g_{i})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.54105pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.54105pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S_{(fg_{i})}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\Longrightarrow\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.39764pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.39764pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{ann\left({\mathscr{F}}\right)_{|D(g_{i})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.39764pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-9.05556pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.33334pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.39764pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{ann\left({\mathscr{F}}\right)_{|D(fg_{i})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.13733pt\raise-9.05556pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.13733pt\raise-24.33334pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-18.48845pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{|D(g_{i})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.30684pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.30684pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{|D(fg_{i})}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad{\rm commutes.}

Let
\lambda\in ann\left({\mathscr{F}}_{|D(g_{i})}\right)
.
It follows

\lambda_{|D(fg_{i})}\in ann\left({\mathscr{F}}_{|D(fg_{i})}\right)~\forall i
.
So, given
m\in\Gamma\left(U,{\mathscr{F}}\right)
we have
(\lambda\cdot m)_{|D(fg_{i})}=0\forall i
. So,
\lambda_{|D(f)}\in ann\left({\mathscr{F}}_{|D(f)}\right)
.
These patch to give a map

ann\left({\mathscr{F}}_{|U}\right)\longrightarrow ann\left({\mathscr{F}}_{|D(f)}\right)

(46)

Now suppose

V=\bigcup_{j=1}^{t}D(f_{j})
. Given
\lambda\in ann\left(\Gamma(U,{\mathscr{F})}\right)
and

m\in\Gamma(V,{\mathscr{F}})
, it follows from above that

(\lambda\cdot m)_{|D(f_{j})}=0~\forall j
. So,
(\lambda\cdot m)_{|V}=0
. So, the map

\Gamma\left(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right)\longrightarrow\Gamma\left(V,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right)
restricts, as follows:

\textstyle{ann\left(\Gamma(U,{\mathscr{F}})\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{ann\left(\Gamma(V,{\mathscr{F}})\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma(U,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma(V,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})}

So,
ann({\mathscr{F}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is an ideal sheaf. Now consider the natural map

\iota:\widetilde{I}\longrightarrow ann\left({\mathscr{F}}\right).

If follows from the affine case that the restriction
\iota_{|D(g)}
is an isomorphism, for all homogeneous
g\in S
. The proof is complete.

Corollary A.7
.

Suppose
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
with
S=A[x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
and
\deg(x_{i})=1
. Assume
A
is noetherian. Let
X\subseteq Y
be open and

{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(X)
. Let
{\mathscr{G}}:=\widetilde{M}\in Coh(Y)
be an extension of
{\mathscr{F}}

(see
[
H77
, Ex II.5.15]
) and

I=ann(M)
. Then
ann({\mathscr{F}})=\widetilde{I}_{|X}
.

Proof.
Follows from (
A.6
).

Definition A.8
.

Let
X
be a noetherian scheme and
{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(X)
. Define

grade({\mathscr{F}})=\inf\left\{r:{\mathscr{E}}xt^{r}\left({\mathscr{F}},{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right)\neq 0\right\}

Proposition A.9
.

Suppose
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
with
S=A[x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
and
\deg(x_{i})=1
. Assume
A
is noetherian. Let
X\subseteq Y
be open,

{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(X)
and
{\mathscr{G}}=\widetilde{M}
where
M
is a finitely generated graded
S
-module, such that
{\mathscr{G}}_{|X}={\mathscr{F}}
(see
[
H77
, Ex II.5.15]
).
Let
I=ann(M)
and
{\mathscr{I}}=\widetilde{I}=ann({\mathscr{G}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
. Then

grade({\mathscr{F}})=grade\left(\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}{{\mathscr{I}}}\right)_{|X}\right)=grade\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{ann{\mathscr{F}}}\right)

Proof.
First note that, for any
{\mathscr{E}},{\mathscr{G}}\in Coh(X)
we have (see
[
H77
, Prop III.6.8]
)

{\mathscr{E}}xt^{i}\left({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{G}}\right)_{x}=Ext^{i}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{x},{\mathscr{G}}_{x}\right)\qquad\forall x\in X,i\geq 0.

Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{l}grade({\mathscr{F}})=\inf\left\{grade\left({\mathscr{F}}_{y}\right):y\in V(I)\cap X\right\}\\
=\inf\left\{grade\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}{{\mathscr{I}}}\right)_{y}:y\in V(I)\cap X\right\}=grade\left(\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}{{\mathscr{I}}}\right)_{|X}\right)\\
\end{array}

This middle equality follows from (
45
).

Proposition A.10
.

Suppose
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
with
S=A[x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
and

\deg(x_{i})=1
. Assume
A
is noetherian. Let
X\subseteq Y
be open and

{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(X)
. Let
{\mathscr{G}}=\widetilde{M}
where
M
is a finitely generated graded
S
-module, such that
{\mathscr{G}}_{|X}={\mathscr{F}}
(see
[
H77
, Ex II.5.15]
). Let
I=ann(M)
and
{\mathscr{I}}=\widetilde{I}=ann({\mathscr{G}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
. Then the following are equivalent:

1.

grade({\mathscr{F}})\geq r.

2.

There is a sequence
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{r}\in I
, of homogeneous elements, such that they induce a regular sequence on
X
. (
This can be read as, if
{\wp}\in X
and
x_{i}\notin{\wp}
, then

\frac{f_{1}}{x_{i}^{\deg(f_{1})}},\frac{f_{2}}{x_{i}^{\deg(f_{2})}},,\ldots,\frac{f_{r}}{x_{i}^{\deg(f_{r})}}

is a
S_{(\wp)}
-regular sequence.
)

Proof.
(
(\ref{596TWO})\Longrightarrow(\ref{592ONE})
:) it follows from (
2
) and (Equation
45
) that
grade({\mathscr{F}}_{x})=depth_{I_{x}}S_{({\wp})}\geq r
and hence

Ext^{i}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{x},{\mathcal{O}}_{x}\right)=0~\forall x\in X,~i\leq r-1
. So,

{\mathscr{E}}xt^{i}({\mathscr{F}},{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=0~\forall i\leq r-1
. Hence
grade({\mathscr{F}})\geq r
. So, (
1
)
is established.

(
(\ref{592ONE})\Longrightarrow(\ref{596TWO})
:)
Suppose
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{p}\in I
is a sequence of homogeneous elements, with
p\leq r-1
, that induce a
S_{({\wp})}
-regular sequence. Let
J=(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{p})
. Let

Ass\left(\frac{S}{J}\right)\cap X=\left\{{\wp}_{1},\ldots,{\wp}_{q}\right\}

Suppose
I\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{q}{\wp}_{i}
. Then
I\subseteq{\wp}={\wp}_{i}
. Then
I_{(\wp})\subseteq{\wp}S_{(\wp)}
. Assume
x_{0}\notin{\wp}
. Since
depth_{I_{({\wp})}}S_{(\wp)}
is well defined, we obtain
depth_{I_{({\wp})}}S_{(\wp)}=p
. Hence
grade\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\wp}\right)=depth_{I_{({\wp})}}S_{(\wp)}=p
.
Hence
Ext^{p}\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\wp},{\mathcal{O}}_{X,{\wp}}\right)\neq 0
. Therefore

{\mathscr{E}}xt^{p}\left({\mathscr{F}},{\mathcal{O}}_{X,}\right)\neq 0
, and
grade({\mathscr{F}})\leq p\leq r-1
, which is a contradiction.

So,
I\not\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{q}{\wp}_{i}
. Let

f_{p+1}\in I-\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{q}{\wp}_{i}\right)
. Then
f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},f_{p+1}\in I
induce a
regular sequence, as required.
The proof is complete.

Proposition A.11
.

Suppose
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
with
S=A[x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
and

\deg(x_{i})=1
. Assume
A
is noetherian. Let
X\subseteq Y
be open and

{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(X)
. Let
{\mathscr{G}}=\widetilde{M}
where
M
is a finitely generated graded
S
-module, such that
{\mathscr{G}}_{|X}={\mathscr{F}}
(see
[
H77
, Ex II.5.15]
).
Assume
X
is Cohen-Macaualy. Then

grade({\mathscr{F}})=co\dim(V(I)\cap X,X)\qquad\qquad{\rm where}\quad I=ann(M).

We extend the definition (
A.3
) and define the grade of a closed subset
of a quasi projective scheme
X
.

Definition A.12
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme of over a noetherian affine scheme
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
and
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset of
X
. Further assume

Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
with
S=A[x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
,

\deg(x_{i})=1
, and
X\subseteq Y
is an open subset.
Define

grade(Z):=grade(Z,X)=grade\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\tilde{I}_{|X}}\right)\qquad{\rm where}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Z=V(I)\cap X,~I\subseteq S\\
{\rm is~a~homogenous~ideal.}\\
\end{array}\right.

This definition is independent of choice of
Y
and
I
.

Proof.
Let
Z=V(I)\cap X=V(J)\cap X
. Considering the irreducible components of
Z
, we have

X\cap\min(I)=X\cap\min(J)=\left\{{\wp}_{1},\ldots,{\wp}_{r}\right\}

So, we can assume
J=\cap_{i=1}^{r}{\wp}_{i}
is reduced and that
I\subseteq J
.
Now

grade\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\tilde{I}_{|X}}\right)=\inf\left\{r:{\mathscr{E}}xt^{r}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\tilde{I}_{|X}},{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\right)\neq 0\right\}=\inf\left\{r:{\mathscr{E}}xt^{r}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{x}}{\tilde{I}_{x}},{\mathcal{O}}_{x}\right)\neq 0,x\in X\right\}

Using (
A.2
) or
[
M70
, Thm 26, pp. 98]
, the above

=\inf\left\{r:{\mathscr{E}}xt^{r}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{x}}{\tilde{J}_{x}},{\mathcal{O}}_{x}\right)\neq 0,x\in X\right\}=grade\left(\frac{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\tilde{J}_{|X}}\right)

The proof is complete.

We recall the definition of perfect modules, before we close this section.

Definition A.13
.

Let
X
be a noetherian scheme and
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset. For
{\mathscr{F}}\in Coh(X)
, we say that
{\mathscr{F}}
is a
perfect module
, if
grade\left({\mathscr{F}}\right)=\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}
. Denote the full subcategory
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)=\left\{{\mathcal{F}}\in Coh_{Z}(X):\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}=grade(Z,X)\right\}
. Note
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)
is a subcategory of the category of perfect modules.

Appendix B
Hermitian theory and Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum

The purpose of this section is to
provide a brief background on jargon of homotopy approach to
K
theory, in the post Quillen
[
Q73
]
era.
Hermitian
K
theory (also called
GW
-theory) is to be seen as part of Algebraic
K
-theory. However, we assume readers are more familiar with
K
-theory, and we will discuss more about
GW
-theory, in this section.
We assume that the readers have some familiarity with the background
[
Q73
,
W83
,
TT90
]
on
K
-theory, or are referred to
[
M23
]
. We also assume that the readers have some familiarity with the category
\mathrm{Sp}
of spectra of pointed topological spaces. Quillen established Algebraic
K
-Theory on the foundation of Exact categories.
In our view, for the esthetics of the narrative, Algebraic
K
-Theory should be a story of exact categories. Formulation of Theorem
1.1
of Quillen supports the same sense of esthetics.
We like to make clean statements within the universe of exact categories, when possible.
However, after
[
Q73
]
, the literature seemed to have drifted away from
K
-theory of exact categories
{\mathscr{E}}
, to that of the category of bounded chain complexes
Ch^{b}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)

[
W83
,
TT90
]
. The theory further drifted into other derivatives of exact categories, like complecial exact categories
[
S11
]
and dg categories
[
S17
]
. While these have enriched the theory, they also have obscured the theory for the broader readership.
The literature misses any common umbrella for all these different derivatives, while
K
-theory of exact categories (inclusive of
GW
-theory) remains the common denominator or, of the fundamental interest.
For this reason, in this section, we recapitulate some of the background on
K
-theory,
mainly of
GW
theory,
and reinterpret them in the context of exact categories. The main goal of this section, is to explain the
Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum
{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)\in\mathrm{BiSp}
of exact categoeies
{\mathscr{E}}_{d}:=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)
with duality.

Let
\underline{\bf eCat}
(resp.
\widetilde{\underline{\bf eCat}}
) denote the category of small
(resp. idempotent complete) exact categories. Likewise,
{\underline{\bf eCat}}D

(resp.
\widetilde{\underline{\bf eCat}}D
) denote the
category of small (resp. idempotent complete) exact categories with duality, over
{\mathbb{Z}}(1/2)
.
We start with the following definition.

Definition B.1
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}_{d}:=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in{\underline{\bf eCat}}D
. Consider the following homotopy fibration:

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 23.08833pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.08833pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.08833pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.08833pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{Q}}^{h}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.37749pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 116.37749pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{Q}}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}{\rm,\quad and}~\quad\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.7986pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf K}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 108.97527pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 108.97527pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{Q}}^{h}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}

Here
{\bf K}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)
denotes the
K
-theory space of
{\mathscr{E}}
and

{\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)
denotes the GW space of
{\mathscr{E}}_{d}
. Ideally, we should denote
{\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right):={\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)
. The latter sequence is a rotation of the first one.
Higher
K
-groups and
GW
-groups are defined by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}K_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)=\pi_{n}\left({\bf K}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\right)\\
GW_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)=\pi_{n}\left({\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\right)\\
\end{array}\right.\qquad\forall n\geq 0.

B.1
K
-theory and
GW
-theory spectrum

The work of Waldhausen
[
W83
]
considered
K
-theory as a spectrum.
We will not discuss the connective
K
-theory spectrum. In this section we give a overview of non connective
K
-theory and
GW
-theory spectrum.
Cone functors

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathfrak{C}_{K}:\widetilde{\underline{\bf eCat}}\longrightarrow\underline{\bf eCat}\\
\mathfrak{C}_{GW}:\widetilde{\underline{\bf eCat}}D\longrightarrow{\underline{\bf eCat}}D\\
\end{array}\right.\quad{\rm together~with}\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathscr{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{K}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)&\forall{\mathscr{E}}\in\widetilde{\underline{\bf eCat}}\\
{\mathscr{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)&\forall{\mathscr{E}}\in\widetilde{\underline{\bf eCat}}D\\
\end{array}\right.

fully faithful exact functors (duality preserving in the latter case), were defined.
Further, for
{\mathscr{E}}\in\widetilde{\underline{\bf eCat}}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{d}:=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in\widetilde{\underline{\bf eCat}}W

1.

The respective quotients

\frac{\mathfrak{C}_{K}{\mathscr{E}}}{{\mathscr{E}}}\in{\underline{\bf eCat}}
and

\frac{\mathfrak{C}_{GW}{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}{{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}\in{\underline{\bf eCat}}D
are defined.

2.

The spaces

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\bf K}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{K}({\mathscr{E}})\right)\cong\star\\
{\bf GW}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)\right)\cong\star\\
\end{array}\right.~{\rm are~contractible}.

(47)

3.

The sequences,

\begin{array}[]{l}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.7986pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf K}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf K}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{K}{{\mathscr{E}}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 107.3278pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 107.3278pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf K}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{C}_{K}{{\mathscr{E}}}}{{\mathscr{E}}}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\\
\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 20.7569pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.7569pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.7569pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.7569pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf GW}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{GW}{{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.47903pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 137.47903pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\bf GW}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{C}_{GW}{{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}}{{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\\
\end{array}\quad{\rm are~homotopy~fibrations}.

(48)

We define suspension functors, as follows.

Definition B.2
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}\in\underline{\bf eCat}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{d}:=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in{\underline{\bf eCat}}D
. Let

\widetilde{{\mathscr{E}}}
and
\widetilde{{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}
denote the respective idempotent completion.
Define suspensions

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{S}}_{K}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right):=\frac{\mathfrak{C}_{K}{\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}}}{\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}}\\
{\mathbb{S}}_{GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right):=\frac{\mathfrak{C}_{K}{\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}}{\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}\\
\end{array}\right.\quad{\rm Inductively,~define}\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{S}}_{K}^{n+1}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right):={\mathbb{S}}_{K}\left(\widetilde{{\mathbb{S}}_{K}^{n}({\mathscr{E}})}\right)\\
{\mathbb{S}}_{GW}^{n+1}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right):={\mathbb{S}}_{GW}^{n}\left(\widetilde{{\mathbb{S}}_{GW}^{n}({\mathscr{E}}_{d})}\right)\\
\end{array}\right.

We define two non connective spectrums, as follows:

1.

The non connective
{\mathbb{K}}
-theory spectrum of
{\mathscr{E}}
is defined as

{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)=\left\{{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right):n\geq 0\right\},\quad{\rm where}\quad{\mathbb{K}}_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)={\bf K}\left(\widetilde{{\mathbb{S}}_{K}^{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)}\right)

The
{\mathbb{K}}
-groups are defined by

{\mathbb{K}}_{p}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)=\pi_{p}\left({\mathbb{K}}{{\mathscr{E}}}\right)\qquad\forall p\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

2.

Define the non connective
GW
-theory spectrum as

\mathfrak{g}{W}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)=\left\{\mathfrak{g}{W}_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right):n\geq 0\right\},\quad{\rm where}\quad\mathfrak{g}{W}_{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)=\mathfrak{g}{W}\left(\widetilde{{\mathbb{S}}_{GW}^{n}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)}\right)

The
\mathfrak{g}{W}
-groups are defined by

\mathfrak{g}{W}_{p}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)=\pi_{p}\left(\mathfrak{g}{W}{{\mathscr{E}}_{d}}\right)\qquad\forall p\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

3.

It follows from (
47
,
48
) that both
{\mathbb{K}}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\in\mathrm{Sp}
and

\mathfrak{g}{W}\left({\mathscr{E}}_{d}\right)\in\mathrm{Sp}
are
\Omega
-spectra. We we have defined two functors:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{K}}:{\underline{\bf eCat}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sp}\\
{\mathfrak{g}}W:{\bf eCat}D\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sp}\\
\end{array}\right.

Thus far, in this section, all the above concerns only exact categories.

B.2
G{\mathcal{W}}
spectrum of dg categories

Thomason
[
TT90
]
started considering
K
-theory of the category of chain complexes of exact categories. This led to consideration of a variety of concepts like BiWaldhausen categories, complicial exact categories, and dg categories.
All these culminated to the
GW
-theory of dg categories in
[
S17
]
. It is worth pointing out that
GW
-theory encapsulates, both
K
-theory and Witt theory. For a dg category

{\mathscr{A}}
the
K
-theory of
{\mathscr{A}}
is given by the
GW
-theory of the hyperbolic category
{\mathcal{H}}\left({\mathscr{A}}\right)
.

Given an exact category
({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi)
with duality
∨
and double dual identification
\varpi
, there is another
exact category
({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},-\varpi)
, usually referred to as the skew duality. So, any theory for
({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi)
, automatically produces a theory for
({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},-\varpi)
. After the work of Thomason
[
TT90
]
, preceded by work of Walhausen
[
W83
]
, attention shifted to the
GW
-Theory of the category of bounded chain complexes
Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}})
, for exact categories
({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi)
with duality. Because of the shift functor, one has one
GW
-theory
GW^{[n]}(Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}}))
, for each

n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. However, these theories are four periodic

GW^{[n]}(Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}}))=GW^{[n+4]}(Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}}))
for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. So, we obtain four
GW
-theory of

Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}})
, while we have only two
GW
-Theory for
({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi)
.

The category
Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}})
of exact categories
\mathscr{E}
can be endowed with the structure of a dg category, denoted by
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}
. This provides justification for
developing
K
-theory and

GW
-theory of dg categories with weak equivalence and duality. Unless stated otherwise, we work with dg categories over
k={\mathbb{Z}}(1/2)
, whenever we work on
GW
-theory.
In addition to the above notations, let

{\bf dgCat}WD
denote the category of small pointed dg
k
-categories
{\mathscr{A}}:=\left({\mathscr{A}},\mathfrak{q},^{\vee},\varpi\right)
with weak equivalences
\mathfrak{q}
, duality
∨
and double dual identification
\varpi
. In our case of
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}
, the quasi isomorphisms are defined to be the weak equivalences.

For
{\mathscr{A}}:=\left({\mathscr{A}},\mathfrak{q},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in{\bf dgCat}WD
, in
[
S17
]
, a spectrum
G{\mathcal{W}}({\mathscr{A}})\in\mathrm{Sp}
was associated in the category
\mathrm{Sp}
of spectrum
of pointed spaces. So, this is a functor

G{\mathcal{W}}:=\{G{\mathcal{W}}(-)_{n}:n\geq 0\}:{\bf dgCat}WD\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sp}

In fact,
G{\mathcal{W}}({\mathscr{A}})_{n}
was defined as the geometric realization of an explicitly defined simplicial category. In fact,
G{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathscr{A}\right)
is a positive
\Omega
-spectrum, which means
G{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathscr{A}\right)_{n}\cong\Omega G{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathscr{A}\right)_{n+1}
,

\forall n\geq 1
.
However, for
{\mathscr{E}}\in{\bf eCat}D
,
G{\mathcal{W}}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)
actually a
\Omega
-spectrum, and

{\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\cong{\bf GW}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\cong\Omega G{\mathcal{W}}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)_{1}

(
See,
[
M23
, Thm 10.1.17, pp 431]
,
[
M23
, Eqn 10.31, pp 449]
).

Now, we have the following agreement proposition.

Proposition B.3

(Agreement)
.

With notations as above, for
{\mathscr{E}}:=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in{\underline{\bf eCat}}D
, with
1/2\in{\mathscr{E}}
, there is a

{\mathfrak{g}}W({\mathscr{E}})\cong G{\mathcal{W}}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\quad homotopy~equivalence~in~\mathrm{Sp}.

(49)

(
We caution that left side is a
\Omega
spectrum and right side is a positive
\Omega
spectrum.
)

Proof.
It follows from uniqueness of
\Omega
spectrum. Recall the formula
[
W78
, pp 107]
,

\left[{\mathbb{S}}^{1}\wedge X,Y\right]\cong\left[X,\Omega Y\right]
, for
X,Y\in{\bf Top}_{\bullet}
. The proof is complete.

B.3
Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum

For
{\mathscr{E}}\in{\underline{\bf eCat}}D
, we provided two descriptions (
49
) of the
GW

spectrum
{\mathfrak{g}}W({\mathscr{E}})\cong G{\mathcal{W}}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\in{\mathrm{Sp}}
. In this section, for

{\mathscr{A}}=\left({\mathscr{A}},\mathfrak{q},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in{\underline{\bf dgCat}}WD
, we associate the Karaiubi Grothendieck-Witt bispectrum
{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{A}}\right)
, which takes values in the category

\mathrm{BiSp}:=\mathrm{BiSp}\left({\mathbb{S}}^{1}-\tilde{\mathbb{S}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}}\right)
of

{\mathbb{S}}^{1}-\tilde{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
-bispectrum.
Here
{\mathbb{S}}^{1}:=\frac{\left[0,1\right]}{0\sim 1}
denotes the
1
-sphere.

We refer to
[
M23
, pp 458-]
,
[
S17
]
for the background on the category
\mathrm{BiSp}
, including definitions of symmetric
K
-spectrum,
{\mathrm{Sp}}\left({\mathscr{C}},K\right)
for monoidal model categories
{\mathscr{C}}:=\left({\mathscr{C}},\otimes,{\mathbbm{1}}\right)
. Among the original sources of all these may be the work of Hovey
[
H01
]
.
We recall some of the jargon and notations:

1.

We used the notation
{\mathrm{Sp}}
for the category of spectrum of pointed topological spaces. Denote the subcategory of symmetric spectra, by
sym{\mathrm{Sp}}
.

2.

We let
\widetilde{\mathrm{Sp}}:=\left(sym{\mathrm{Sp}},\wedge_{{\mathbb{S}}},{\mathbb{S}}\right)
denote the category symmetric spectrum, where
{\mathbb{S}}=\left\{\wedge^{n}{\mathbb{S}}^{1}:n\geq 0\right\}
is the ring spectrum, which acts as the unit.
With
{\mathscr{C}}=\left({\bf Top}_{\bullet},\wedge,{\mathbb{S}}^{0}\right)
, and
K={\mathbb{S}}^{1}
we have

\widetilde{\mathrm{Sp}}:={\mathrm{Sp}}\left({\mathscr{C}},K\right)
(see
[
M23
, pp 458]
). Note
\widetilde{\mathrm{Sp}}
is a symmetric monoidal model category
[
M23
, pp 460]
.

3.

Now, we repeat the same process, starting with

{\mathscr{C}}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{Sp}}:=\left(sym{\mathrm{Sp}},\wedge_{{\mathbb{S}}},{\mathbb{S}}\right)
. Let
{\mathbb{S}}_{{\mathrm{Sp}}}^{1}=\left\{{\mathbb{S}}^{1},{\mathbb{S}}^{2},{\mathbb{S}}^{3},\ldots\right\}\in sym{\mathrm{Sp}}
be the sphere spectrum, and let

K=\widetilde{{\mathbb{S}}_{{\mathrm{Sp}}}^{1}}\in sym{\mathrm{Sp}}
be the cofibrant replacement of

{\mathbb{S}}_{{\mathrm{Sp}}}^{1}
. This means
K
is cofibrant and
K\longrightarrow{\mathbb{S}}_{{\mathrm{Sp}}}^{1}
is trivial fibration. Now define the monoidal model category of bispctra (see
[
M23
, pp 461]
)

\mathrm{BiSp}:=\mathrm{BiSp}\left({\mathbb{S}}^{1}-\tilde{\mathbb{S}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}}\right):={\mathrm{Sp}}\left(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Sp}}},K\right)

So, the objects of
\mathrm{BiSp}
are symmetric
\widetilde{{\mathrm{Sp}}}
-spectrum,
\left\{{\mathcal{X}}_{0},{\mathcal{X}}_{1},\ldots\right\}
, where
{\mathcal{X}}_{i}\in sym{\mathrm{Sp}}
.

Write
K=\tilde{\mathbb{S}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
. Given
{\bf X}=\left\{{\mathcal{X}}_{0},{\mathcal{X}}_{1},\ldots\right\}\in\mathrm{BiSp}
following homotopy groups are defined:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\pi_{n}\left({\bf X}\right)=\lim_{k}\left[K^{n+k},{\mathcal{X}}_{k}\right]_{Ho\mathrm{Sp}^{\Sigma}}&Naive~homotopy\\
\pi_{n}^{t}\left({\bf X}\right)=\lim_{k}\left[\Sigma K^{n},{\bf X}\right]_{Ho\mathrm{BiSp}}&True~homotopy\\
\end{array}\right.\qquad\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}

(50)

where
\Sigma K^{n}=\left\{K^{n+k}:k=0,1,2,\ldots\right\}\in\mathrm{BiSp}
.
Under suitable conditions, these two groups coincide
[
S17
, pp 1840]
.

Now we proceed to define Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
bispectrum.

Definition B.4
.

Let
k
be a commutative ring, with
1/2\in k
. Let
{\underline{\bf dgCat}}WD_{k}
denote the category of dg
k
-categories with weak equivalences and duality.

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm A~cone~functor}&{\bf C}:{\bf dg}CatWD_{k}\longrightarrow{\bf dg}CatWD_{k}~and\\
{\rm a~suspension~functor}&\mathfrak{s}:{\bf dg}CatWD_{k}\longrightarrow{\bf dg}CatWD_{k}\\
\end{array}\right.

were constructed, on the category
{\bf dg}CatWD_{k}
.
For
\left({\mathscr{A}},^{\vee},\mathfrak{q},\varpi\right)\in{\bf dg}CatWD_{k}
, and integer
n\geq 0
,
one defines

{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{A}})_{n}:=G{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathfrak{s}^{n}{\mathscr{A}}\right)\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Sp}}

(51)

in the category

\widetilde{\mathrm{Sp}}
of symmetric spectra. The bispectrum is defined, as a functor

{\mathbb{G}}W:{\bf dgCat}WD_{k}\longrightarrow\mathrm{BiSp}\quad sending\quad{\mathscr{A}}\mapsto{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{A}}):=\left\{{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{A}})_{n}:n\geq 0\right\}.

Consequently, for
{\mathscr{E}}\in{\bf eCat}D_{k}
, one can associate

{\mathscr{E}}\mapsto{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\in\mathrm{BiSp}.\quad{\rm Note}\quad{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)=\left\{G{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathfrak{s}^{n}{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right):n\geq 0\right\}

Following are some observation on
GW
invariants of exact categories with duality.

Remark B.5
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in\underline{\bf eCat}
.

1.

The
\mathfrak{g}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Sp}}=\left(sym{\mathrm{Sp}},\wedge_{{\mathbb{S}}},{\mathbb{S}}\right)
. This follows from the fact the spectrum
\mathfrak{g}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)
is defined by iterated suspension.

2.

{\mathscr{E}}
can be considered as a dg category with weak equivalence (isomorphisms) and duality, concentrated at degree zero
[
M23
, pp 435]
.

3.

Since
\mathfrak{s}
is ring, it is easy to see that

\mathfrak{s}^{n}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}
is defined, and is an exact category with duality, where split exact sequences are defined to be exact. Define

{\mathfrak{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)_{n}=\mathfrak{g}{W}\left(\mathfrak{s}^{n}\otimes{\mathscr{E}}\right),\quad and\quad{\mathfrak{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)=\left\{{\mathfrak{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)_{n}:n\geq 0\right\}

The following reconciles two bispectrum of exact categories.

Proposition B.6
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in\underline{\bf eCat}
.
Then

1.

{\mathfrak{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\in\mathrm{BiSp}
is a bispectrum.

2.

There is zig zag stable equivalence

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 19.63197pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.63197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathfrak{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.54167pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.63197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.63197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{G}}W\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad in\quad\mathrm{BiSp}.

Proof.
The proof is elementary, which we skip. Main point is that an exact category
{\mathscr{E}}
can be considered as a dg category, concentrated at degree zero. We plan to write a complete exposition somewhere else
[
M24p
]
.

We summarize some of the above notations and introduce some more, for
n\geq 0
, as follows.

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathfrak{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})_{n}:={\mathfrak{g}}W\left(\mathfrak{s}^{n}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\in sym\mathrm{Sp},\quad and\\
{\mathfrak{G}}W({\mathscr{E}}):=\left\{{\mathfrak{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})_{n}:n\geq 0\right\}\in\mathrm{BiSp}\\
{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})_{n}:={\mathfrak{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})_{n},~{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})^{+}:={\mathfrak{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})\in\mathrm{BiSp}\\
{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})^{-}:={\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},-\varpi\right)\in\mathrm{BiSp}\\
\end{array}\right.

(52)

It also follows that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})\cong{\mathbb{G}}W^{[4n]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}})\\
{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})^{-}\cong{\mathbb{G}}W^{[4n+2]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}})\\
\end{array}\right.\qquad\forall n\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

While we obtain two bispectrum
{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})
,
{\mathbb{G}}W({\mathscr{E}})^{-}
directly from an exact category,
{\mathscr{E}}=\left({\mathscr{E}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)
, the shifted bispectrum

{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}})\cong{\mathbb{G}}W^{[n+4]}({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}})
is four periodic.
Following (
50
),
define the Karoubi
{\mathbb{G}}W
-groups

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}\left({\mathscr{A}}\right)=\pi_{p}^{t}\left({\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{A}}\right)\right)&\forall{\mathscr{A}}\in{\underline{\bf dgCat}}WD_{k}\\
{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)=\pi_{p}^{t}\left({\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\right)\cong\pi_{p}^{t}\left({\mathbb{G}}W^{[4n]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\right)&\forall{\mathscr{E}}\in{\underline{\bf eCat}}D\\
{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)^{-}=\pi_{p}^{t}\left({\mathbb{G}}W\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)^{-}\right)\cong\pi_{p}^{t}\left({\mathbb{G}}W^{[4n+2]}\left({\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}\right)\right)&\forall{\mathscr{E}}\in{\underline{\bf eCat}}D\\
\end{array}\right.\quad\forall p,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

More explicitly, we have the following.

Corollary B.7
.

Let
{\mathscr{C}}=\left({\mathscr{C}},^{\vee},\varpi\right)\in{\bf eCat}D_{\mathbb{Z}}
. As usual, denote

\forall k\in{\mathbb{Z}},~{\mathbb{G}}W_{k}({\mathscr{E}})=\pi_{k}\left({\mathbb{G}}W_{k}({\mathscr{E}})\right)
denotes the homotopy group.
Then

{\mathbb{G}}W_{p}({\mathscr{E}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf GW}_{p}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)&p\geq 1\\
{\bf GW}_{0}\left(\widetilde{{\bf D}^{b}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)}\right)&p=0\\
{\bf GW}_{0}\left(\widetilde{{\bf D}^{b}\left(\mathfrak{s}^{-p}{\mathscr{E}}\right)}\right)&p\leq-1\\
\end{array}\right.

Here
\forall k\geq 0,~{\bf GW}_{k}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)=\pi_{k}\left({\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)\right)
denote the
GW
-groups,
while
{\bf GW}\left({\mathscr{E}}\right)
denotes the
{GW}
-space.

Proof.
It follows directly from
[
S17
, pp 1799]
,
[
M23
]
.

Appendix C
Notations

In this section we establish some standard, and some not-so-standard notations. We freely use some of the notations from
[
H77
]
, which I consider standard by now.

Notation C.1
.

Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over a noetherian affine scheme
A
, and

Z\subseteq X
be a closed subset and
U=X-Z
. We assume that
grade(Z)=k
. In the context of
{\mathbb{G}}W
-theory,
{\mathscr{L}}
will denote a fixed invertible sheaf. As is standard,

QCoh(X)
,
Coh(X)
,
{\mathscr{V}}(X)
, respectively, denote the category of Quasi Coherent,
Coherent, locally free sheaves over

X
.

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Denote~d=\dim X,~k=gradeZ\quad{\rm(see~Definition~\ref{GradCloQA})}\\
Coh^{Z}(X)=\{M\in Coh(X):\mathrm{Supp}\left(M\right)\subseteq Z\}\\
\forall M\in Coh(X),~{\rm denote}~\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}{{\mathscr{F}}}:=projective~(locally~free)~dimension\\
{\mathbb{M}}(X)=\{M\in Coh(X):\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}(M)<\infty\}\\
{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)=\left\{M\in{\mathbb{M}}(X):\mathrm{Supp}\left(M\right)\subseteq Z\right\}\\
C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X)=\left\{M\in{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X):{\dim_{{\mathscr{V}(X)}}}(M)=k\right\}\quad{\rm see~(\ref{defPerModd})}\\
{\rm Usually,}~{\mathscr{E}}(X)\subseteq QCoh(X)~{\rm would~denote~an~exact~subcategory}\\
{\rm Examples:~}{\mathscr{E}}(X)=Coh(X),Coh^{Z}(X),{\mathbb{M}}(X),C{\mathbb{M}}^{Z}(X),~{and~others}\\
\end{array}\right.

We continue:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\rm For~such~an~exact~category,}\\
Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}}(X))~{\rm denotes~the~category~of~bounded~complexes,~with~objects~in~}{\mathscr{E}}(X)\\
Ch^{b}_{Z}({\mathscr{E}}(X))=\left\{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}}(X)):\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)_{|X-Z}~is~exact\right\}\quad{\rm the~full~subcategory.}\\
{\bf dg}{\mathscr{E}}(X)~{\rm denotes~the~dg~category~corresponding~to~}Ch^{b}({\mathscr{E}}(X))\\
{\bf dg}_{Z}{\mathscr{E}}(X)~{\rm denotes~the~dg~category~corresponding~to~}Ch^{b}_{Z}({\mathscr{E}}(X))\\
{\bf D}^{b}({\mathscr{E}}(X))={\rm Bounded~Derived~Category~of~exact~categories}~{\mathscr{E}}(X)\\
{\mathscr{D}}^{b}_{Z}({\mathscr{E}}(X))=\left\{{\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\in{\bf D}^{b}({\mathscr{E}}(X)):\left({\mathscr{F}}_{\bullet}\right)_{|X-Z}~is~exact\right\}\quad{\rm the~full~subcategory.}\\
\end{array}\right.

For future use, we extend the list (
C.1
), as follows

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\bf P}erf(X)\subseteq Ch(QCoh(X))~{\rm denote~the~category~of~perfect~complexes}\\
{\bf P}erf_{Z}(X)=\left\{M_{\bullet}\in{\bf P}erf(X):\left({\mathcal{H}}_{n}\left(M_{\bullet}\right)\right)_{|U}=0~\forall~n\right\}~{\rm~be~the~full~subcategory.}\\
\end{array}\right.

For a larger part of this article, we work with the following setup.

Notation C.2
.

Let
A
be a noetherian commutative ring. Let
X
be a quasi projective scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}\left(A\right)
.
We will be working under the following setup.

1.

Let
Y=\mathrm{Proj}\left(S\right)
for some graded ring
S=A[x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
with
\deg(x_{i})=1
.

2.

We assume
X\subseteq Y
is an open subset. Assume
\dim X=d
.
